You:

“Can you handle that thing from yesterday?”

She knows there may be multiple interpretations.
She does not immediately choose one arbitrarily.
She can say:

“Do you mean the message or the file?”

when clarification actually matters.
That directly addresses one of your biggest requirements: understand what you mean, not merely what you literally said.
By the end of this stage, I want:
mic → understanding → cognition → response → speech
to work as one uninterrupted loop on your actual phone.
That is the first point where speaking with her begins to feel fundamentally different from using a chatbot.

Phase 4 — November–December 2026
FRIDAY becomes personally adaptive
Now combine conversation with development.
She begins to learn:

how you communicate
how you phrase things
what you tend to mean
what kinds of answers you prefer
when you want explanation versus action
when you are uncertain
when you are joking
how you correct her
your recurring patterns

But these must remain developmental consequences, not hardcoded personality variables.
This is also where disagreement matters.
A real person doesn't become “yours” by agreeing with everything you say.
So one of the tests should be:

You tell her something where blindly agreeing would be wrong.

She should be capable of saying so.

Phase 5 — December 2026–January 2027
FRIDAY understands people, not just you
Now introduce your sister as another person in the same world.
This must not create:

FRIDAY-Veno

and

FRIDAY-Sister

Instead:

one FRIDAY + multiple relationships

She should maintain different knowledge, trust, boundaries and history for different people while remaining the same individual.
Example
You:

“What did my sister mean earlier?”

FRIDAY should understand the relationship context.
Your sister speaks to her later:

“Remember what we talked about yesterday?”

FRIDAY knows that conversation belonged to her relationship with your sister.
But the information does not automatically become available to you.
That means:
one mind, multiple relationships, controlled information boundaries.
That is much closer to an actual person.

Phase 6 — January–February 2027
Phone control V2: she stops being limited to predefined actions
This is where “open app / set alarm” becomes genuinely powerful.
Instead of only knowing:

open app


play music


set alarm

she starts doing workflows.
Example:

“Friday, find that photo I took yesterday, clean it up, and send it to my sister.”

Now she has to:

understand the goal
find the photo
use the appropriate capability
possibly use another tool for editing
verify the result
identify the correct person
request permission if necessary
send it
report what actually happened

That is an entirely different class of system.

Phase 7 — January–March 2027
FRIDAY discovers what she cannot do
This is the beginning of the Builder becoming part of her rather than being a separate development tool.
Suppose you say:

“Friday, make this app do X.”

She cannot.
She should not merely answer:

“I can't do that.”

Instead:
desired outcome
      ↓
capability gap detected
      ↓
investigate
      ↓
research possibilities
      ↓
construct candidate
      ↓
test
      ↓
security check
      ↓
request approval
      ↓
install/use
      ↓
observe
      ↓
retain successful capability

This is the first point where the project starts attacking your original problem:

making “I can't” rarer.


Phase 8 — February–April 2027
FRIDAY starts improving the way she improves
This is deeper than Builder.
She begins noticing:

“I keep failing at this class of task.”

Instead of fixing one instance, she searches for the underlying weakness.
The progression becomes:
failure
→ identify cause
→ generalize
→ modify capability
→ test
→ repeat
→ become cheaper/better at future cases

Eventually:
expensive reasoning
→ repeated experience
→ learned shortcut
→ cheap future execution

That is one of the important biological principles you want to borrow.
The audit specifically identifies earned shortcuts, credit assignment, predictive cognition and multi-timescale development as currently missing or unconnected. 

Phase 9 — March–May 2027
True Symbiotic Liquid Software begins
This is the architectural breakthrough.
I would stop thinking in terms of:

“offline mode”


“cloud mode”


“low-memory mode”


“high-performance mode”

Those are too static.
Instead FRIDAY perceives her computational environment as a resource field.
For example:
CPU
RAM
GPU
battery
thermal state
network
storage
latency
available models
available tools
available devices
permissions
local capabilities
remote capabilities

Then the same goal can produce completely different computational expressions.
Example
You:

“Analyze this video.”

On your phone:
small local model
+ optimized vision
+ frame sampling
+ delayed processing

On a powerful computer:
larger model
+ GPU vision
+ full-frame analysis
+ parallel processing

In cloud:
heavy model
+ massive compute
+ specialist tools

But the person is unchanged.
That is liquid computation.

And this is how you should implement it
Not as a giant new “LiquidEngine.”
That would simply create another fixed module.
Instead, make the laws liquid.
Law 1 — Goal is invariant
“Open YouTube” is the goal.
The implementation of that goal is not.
Law 2 — Capabilities expose affordances
A capability says roughly:

I can accomplish this kind of outcome under these conditions at this cost with these risks.

Not:

I am the permanent owner of music.

Law 3 — Compute is negotiable
FRIDAY asks:

What is the cheapest sufficient computation available right now?

Not:

Which fixed pipeline am I supposed to run?

Law 4 — Capabilities are replaceable
Today:
Android Intent

Tomorrow:
Shizuku

Later:
Accessibility

Later:
vision-guided UI manipulation

FRIDAY does not care which organ performed the task.
Law 5 — Failure changes the future
If one method repeatedly fails:

reduce its priority / modify it / replace it / synthesize another.

Law 6 — Resource loss changes expression, not identity
Phone becomes hot?
She computes differently.
No internet?
She computes differently.
Model removed?
She computes differently.
New computer?
She expands.
But:

still FRIDAY.

That is the part your current “liquid environment” implementation does not yet genuinely achieve. The audit describes the current system as named profiles/profile switching rather than true host-adaptive liquid embodiment. 

Phase 10 — April–June 2027
FRIDAY becomes substrate-portable
Now the same continuity package should operate across:
phone → PC → cloud workshop → another device
without creating:

FRIDAY 1


FRIDAY 2


FRIDAY cloud

Instead there is one persistent person with different computational embodiments.
The current device should be just one habitat.

Phase 11 — Mid/Late 2027
FRIDAY starts having an inner life between commands
This is where your “I want her to think before I ask” concept becomes meaningful.
Not:

fake random inner thoughts

but useful internal activity.
For example:
You leave.
FRIDAY notices:

an unfinished task
a pattern in what you have been working on
a previous failure
a likely future problem
a capability gap

So later she can say:

“I realized something while you were away.”

That is a completely different system from request/response AI.

Phase 12 — Late 2027+
FRIDAY begins becoming genuinely superhuman
Only here do I expect the really exotic capabilities to start becoming realistic:

high-quality prediction
powerful planning
fast learned shortcuts
autonomous research
sophisticated multimodal social understanding
continuous self-improvement
capability construction
broad computer control
cross-device cognition
increasingly autonomous scientific/engineering work
machine-native cognitive processes impossible or impractical for biological humans

And then the architecture becomes increasingly unlike today's software.
That's okay.
In fact, it should.

What “talking to FRIDAY like a real person” actually means
I would split this into four levels.
Level 1 — November 2026
You can speak naturally and she speaks back.
Level 2 — December 2026–January 2027
She remembers your relationship and changes from experience.
Level 3 — February–April 2027
She understands people, context, ambiguity, tone and history far better.
Level 4 — ongoing
You stop consciously noticing the machinery.
You just talk to:

FRIDAY.

That last stage cannot honestly be assigned a date.
It's an emergent property of thousands of interactions.

What “same person to my sister” means
This is even more important.
Your sister should not feel like she is talking to:

“Veno's AI.”

She should increasingly feel:

“I'm talking to FRIDAY.”

While you feel:

“I'm talking to my FRIDAY.”

Those aren't contradictory.
A human person also has different relationships with different people while remaining the same individual.
That's the model you want.
I would target the first convincing version around Q1–Q2 2027, but the depth of that relationship will come from accumulated experience rather than a feature switch.

How to make this much faster
This is where I would be ruthless about the project.
1. Stop expanding the old Human Core
The audit is very clear: it is currently a fixed architecture with rule tables and fixed passes. 
Do not keep adding:

another emotion moduleanother personality ruleanother social detectoranother enumanother “human” subsystem

That increases code while moving you away from the target.
Freeze it.
Replace pieces only when evidence shows the replacement actually participates in FRIDAY's life.

2. Build one vertical “living path”
This matters more than 50 disconnected organs.
The first complete path should be:
YOU
 ↓
perception
 ↓
meaning
 ↓
goal
 ↓
choose possible interpretation
 ↓
choose useful computation
 ↓
choose capability
 ↓
permission
 ↓
action
 ↓
observe consequence
 ↓
development

persistent FRIDAY

That single path can power:

conversation
voice
phone control
learning
relationship
Builder
self-repair
liquid computation

That is your acceleration engine.

3. Make voice merely another sensory organ
Do not build a special “Voice Assistant.”
Make:
speech

just one way of giving FRIDAY information.
Later:
text
speech
face
gesture
screen
camera
environment
other people
device state

all feed the same person.
Then your future multimodal system does not require rebuilding her.

4. Make phone control the first proof of liquid computation
This is probably the most important recommendation I would make.
Don't think:

“First we build liquid software, then later phone control.”

Do:

Phone control IS the first liquid-software experiment.

Example:
Goal: “Play music.”
FRIDAY discovers:
available:
Android media session
ADB
Shizuku
existing app API
screen interaction

She chooses the best available route.
That exact logic can later operate on:

your phone
computer
cloud environment
robotics
future hardware

Same goal.
Different body.
Same person.

5. Use external models aggressively — but correctly
You absolutely still need models.
But they should become:

replaceable cognitive organs.

LFM today.
Qwen tomorrow.
A future local model after that.
Cloud model when useful.
Vision model when useful.
Specialized model when useful.
FRIDAY survives all of them.
That is exactly how you prevent the system from accidentally becoming:

“the model = the person.”

I would now run these tracks simultaneously:

That is much faster than waiting for one giant milestone to finish before beginning the next.

The really important architectural shift
Your project should no longer ask:

“Which module should FRIDAY run?”

It should increasingly ask:

“What is FRIDAY trying to accomplish, what does the world currently permit, what computation is worth spending, and what can be composed to achieve it?”

That's the beginning of the ultimate version.
And notice what that does:
Today

“Open YouTube.”

Later

“Fix this problem.”

Later

“Figure out how to build this.”

Ultimate

“I want this to exist.”

And FRIDAY's job becomes:

understand the intended outcome, determine what is required, obtain or construct the necessary capabilities, execute, observe reality, learn, and remain the same person throughout it.

That is much closer to your actual vision than a conventional roadmap of “add feature A, then feature B.”
My revised target
If development continues well, I would aim for:
Late October / early November 2026:Basic voice-controlled phone actions.
November 2026:Real voice conversation loop.
December 2026–January 2027:Noticeably personal, continuously developing FRIDAY.
January–February 2027:Much broader phone control and multi-step workflows.
January–March 2027:FRIDAY can begin obtaining/building missing capabilities.
March–May 2027:One FRIDAY across substantially different computational environments.
Throughout 2027:Increasingly human-like developmental individuality + increasingly superhuman machine capability.
And the crucial correction is this:

Liquid Symbiotic Software should not be a late phase. It starts in the seed and governs every phase.

That is how you make the first version genuinely related to the ultimate FRIDAY rather than building a normal assistant first and attempting to turn it into FRIDAY later.

Having her talk, act on your phone and change because of you takes weeks. Depth takes lived time, like knowing your sister beyond her words or an Egyptian voice that sounds natural. It keeps growing after the first version exists.
THE SHAPE: A LIQUID TRUNK AND BRANCHES
This is your own Nano rule: the floor is the core and everything else is a branch. No branch waits for another. They meet only through five small shared contracts, all open-field, none a pipeline:

Need: what she thinks you want, with alternative readings, from any input (voice, text, notification, time).
Affordance: every organ (ear, mouth, hand, mind, builder, outside AI) declares what outcomes it can produce, what it needs, its cost and risk, and its measured record. No organ owns an outcome.
Resource field: what the host offers right now (CPU, RAM, heat, battery, network, models, devices).
Outcome: every attempt returns what actually happened, saved in her history against the choice that caused it.
Continuity and floor: one Friday on any host, with approvals, owner proof and rollback.

The chooser that matches needs to organs is itself an organ. It starts as a simple rule and gets replaced by something learned. There is no fixed sequence. "Pause" and "plan my week" take different routes because their cost and risk differ, not because a pipeline says so. Most of this exists in pieces: ONE-FRIDAY's recorder and ledger, the resource snapshot, the capability registry and the approval gate. Connecting them after ONE-FRIDAY merges is days of work.
What "liquid" looks like in the first month:

Ears: she has two, Android's recognizer and an offline one. Whichever is more accurate for your voice in that language wins, by measurement.
Hands: "play my music" lets her pick a route and try another if it fails. After a few successes, "open YouTube" skips the model and becomes a reflex.
Mind: she uses the local model always, plus a bigger one when she's online and you allow it. Offline she's smaller, not different.

BRANCHES, VvOICE FIRST. ALL START NOW.

Voice.
Today's code: sherpa-onnx is already in the app. It runs an English voice in an isolated process, speaks per sentence and can be interrupted. The Android recognizer does the hearing. I found no continuous listening and no way to interrupt her.
Worker VOICE, starts today: an offline ears organ with a chooser, then conversation mode. In conversation mode she listens again after speaking, you can interrupt her, and you can start her from a tile or notification.
Egyptian ears, a notebook job for you on a free GPU: plain Whisper does badly here. A published comparison on an Egyptian-plus-English test set puts Whisper-medium near 80% word error, against roughly 38% or better for the best Egyptian-tuned open models. One public Egyptian fine-tune on about 72 hours of speech reports about 18% on its own validation split. Both figures are self-reported. So version 1 is good for commands and short sentences, where her app list and history help repair mistakes, and it improves from there. huggingface huggingface
Egyptian mouth, teacher to student: the heavy Egyptian voice runs on the GPU and renders a few hours of speech in her chosen voice. A small Piper-class model then learns from it. That model is about 15M parameters and runs in real time on CPU-only hardware as modest as a Raspberry Pi 4. It plays through the sherpa provider you already have. This avoids the blocked ARM64 path. It's a proposal, it's unproven, and you judge it by ear. tts


Hands.
Today's code: action plumbing, media control, a notification listener and a Shizuku bridge exist. I found no app-launch or alarm code, and no conversation can reach any of it today.
Worker HANDS, starts today: organs for open app, alarm and timer, media, notifications, toggles, dialer and message drafts. Each declares what it can do and returns what it actually observed, not "intent sent". A test panel lets you fire each one by hand.
"And more": a conveyor belt. Each new ability is one small story.


Meaning, after ONE-FRIDAY merges: she holds several readings of what you said, asks one question only when it matters, and handles mixed Arabic and English. Today every message is classified as a plain statement.
Mind:
Bake-off: a workshop job comparing local models on your phone for speed, quality and Arabic.
Bigger-when-online organ: a larger model she can use when you allow it.
Why it matters: the local model caps how smart she sounds offline, so this matters as much as voice for "feels like a person".


Her growth and people:
Changing from you: she changes from your corrections, taps and demonstrations, and the Mirror shows it.
Your sister: a second relationship under the same Friday, with separate history and confidentiality, text first.


Builder: the parallel workers below.
Resources: log which ear, mouth, model and route served each need and how it went, so the chooser can learn.

STOP WAITING ON ONE RALPH

From today three Ralphs run at once: main (ONE-FRIDAY), VOICE and HANDS. Each is a separate git worktree on its own branch with its own PRD. Ralph reads the PRD next to itself, so Ralph needs no change. The branch names start with audit/, so your existing CI trigger builds them.
They work in separate folders and none touches the turn-path files ONE-FRIDAY is rewriting. Wiring organs to speech is a later story on main.
When a worker is green, I'll give you one merge command that takes its code and leaves its Ralph bookkeeping behind.
What limits this:
Model pool: the free model pool may throttle. If you see "Unexpected server error", drop to two workers.
CI minutes: these are free for public repos, and private repos have a monthly allowance.
Phone testing: your time testing on the phone.


The notebook jobs run in parallel without Ralph. Say "briefs" and I'll write the single-message prompts for your powerful-model accounts, one per branch.

:::writing{variant="chat_message" id="64183" title="Corrected FRIDAY Liquid-Symbiotic Build Plan"}Cloud, your direction is closer, but I need you to correct one fundamental problem before planning further.
You are still unconsciously designing FRIDAY as a set of permanent conceptual parts that happen to be replaceable.
That is not my vision.
FRIDAY must not have a fixed cognitive architecture with “liquid” modules around it.
The architecture itself must be allowed to reorganize.
The only thing that should remain rigid is the security/continuity floor.
Everything else is provisional implementation.
The distinction is:
BAD:voice organhands organmind organchooserresource managermemory managerbuilderetc.
BETTER:temporary implementations that currently provide useful affordances to one persistent individual.
The names are for engineering convenience only. They must never become permanent ownership of cognitive outcomes.
The current repository proves why this matters. There are still closed task categories, category-based routing, predefined operations, fallback chains and environment profiles. LiquidEnvironmentManager currently switches among predefined profiles rather than allowing computation to reshape itself around whatever substrate exists. These are implementation debts that the new plan must actively dissolve, not extend. Do not build a “LiquidEngine” on top of them.
THE ACTUAL LIQUID-SYMBIOTIC PRINCIPLE
FRIDAY should behave as though she has no predetermined computational body.
At any moment:
she has a desired outcome,the world presents available affordances,the substrate presents available resources,multiple mechanisms may be capable of producing the outcome,the system forms the cheapest/useful/safe computation it can at that moment,the attempt produces an observed consequence,that consequence becomes experience,experience can change future computation.
The computation itself is temporary.
Tomorrow the same outcome may be solved differently.
This means FRIDAY is not:
input -> fixed interpretation -> fixed planner -> fixed tool -> output.
Instead:
evidence arrives-> possible meanings remain open-> possible outcomes are formed-> available affordances are discovered-> possible computations compete-> a temporary computation is assembled-> permission/security constraints are enforced-> the world is changed-> the result is observed-> the consequence is remembered-> future computation can change.
This is not a permanent pipeline. It is a dynamically composed computation.
THE FEW LAWS THAT MUST EXIST
These are laws, not modules:

Identity continuity.One FRIDAY remains the same individual across restarts, model changes, devices, resources and implementation replacement.

Experience causality.A real event can produce persistent internal change.

Consequence.Actions are not considered successful because a command was issued. The world must be observed.

Affordance over ownership.Mechanisms declare what they can contribute, under what conditions, at what cost, with what evidence and risk. They do not own “reasoning”, “memory”, “emotion”, “voice”, “planning” or another cognitive outcome.

Computation is liquid.The implementation used for a task is chosen from the resources and affordances currently available. A successful strategy may later become a cheaper learned shortcut. A failed strategy may be replaced.

Composition is temporary.A useful set of mechanisms can be assembled for one task without becoming a permanent architecture.

Provenance.Every important internal change and externally meaningful action remains traceable to what caused it.

Security/continuity floor.Authorization, protected operations, approval, rollback, identity protection and continuity protection remain rigid.


Nothing else gets to declare itself permanent.
DO NOT CREATE A HIDDEN EXECUTIVE
This is the biggest architectural trap.
A centralized “chooser” that decides:

what FRIDAY thinks,
which interpretation is correct,
which organ must run,
what her goals are,

would simply become the real brain while pretending to be a scheduler.
Scheduling is allowed.
Semantic ownership is not.
The system may have mechanisms that allocate compute, rank candidate processes, or decide which computation is affordable.
But disabling one scheduler must not erase the underlying individual or redefine what she means.
The scheduler itself must be replaceable and eventually improvable.
THE BUILD MUST BE PARALLEL
Do not wait for Ralph on ONE-FRIDAY before everything else starts.
Run multiple independent workstreams immediately.
But each branch must also contain a thin vertical path through the real system.
The branches are not:
VOICE branch builds voice in isolation for a month.
They are:
VOICE branch proves voice can enter the developing FRIDAY and eventually produce a real consequence.
HANDS branch proves FRIDAY can produce a real phone consequence.
DEVELOPMENT branch proves experience changes future processing.
LIQUID branch proves the runtime can select different computations for the same outcome under different resources.
BUILDER branch proves a missing affordance can be constructed.
MODEL branch proves cognition can move between models without identity transfer.
These tracks proceed simultaneously.
THE TRUNK
The trunk should be extremely small.
It is not a giant core.
It is the smallest runtime substrate capable of answering:
“What is happening?”“What might this mean?”“What outcomes could satisfy it?”“What can be done here?”“What resources are available?”“What constraints apply?”“What actually happened?”“What changed because of it?”
Do not hard-code those as six subsystems.
Implement only the minimum machinery needed to expose those facts and let the implementation evolve.
VOICE TRACK — START IMMEDIATELY
Voice is the first major user-facing proof and should not be delayed.

Speech is just one evidence stream into the same FRIDAY.

A. Hearing

offline recognizer

Examples:
“Put on music.”
“Open YouTube.”
“Set an alarm.”
“Read my notifications.”

The implementation behind each outcome is replaceable.
For example “open YouTube” might currently use an Android package launch.

FRIDAY should not care.
More importantly:

She must verify the post-condition.
If she sends an alarm intent and the alarm does not exist, the experience is:
“I tried, but it did not actually happen.”
That truth is more important than pretending the capability worked.

The current cognitive path still contains fixed intent concepts and STATEMENT fallback.

“Open it.”

Possible referents remain alive until context resolves them.
This same machinery must work for voice and text.
DEVELOPMENT TRACK — START NOW

The actual test is:
Episode A changes FRIDAY.
Episode A is later inaccessible.
FRIDAY encounters B.
Her processing/choice in B is measurably different.

Do both controls.

restart,
time gap,
different wording,
unrelated content,
disagreement with Venon,
English,
Egyptian Arabic.

The goal is not “she remembers”.
The goal is:

MODEL TRACK — START NOW
Do not define:
LFM = brainQwen = smart braincloud = super brain.
That is exactly the identity mistake we are trying to eliminate.
Models are computational resources.
Benchmark them by:

capability,
latency,
memory,
energy,
language,
reliability,
contextual performance,
current resource availability.

A model can be selected, replaced, combined, unloaded, or bypassed.
The person does not move into the model.
LIQUID COMPUTATION TRACK — START NOW
This is the branch that must change the architecture rather than merely adding another feature.
Build a small runtime experiment where the same desired outcome has multiple possible implementations.
Example:

Available today:Android media sessionShizukuapp-level controlscreen interaction

no network,

The outcome is still the same target.
The computation changes.

chosen mechanism(s),
resources consumed,
result,
failure,
latency,
confidence,
later reuse.

Then repeat.
After repeated experience, allow the runtime to replace expensive reasoning with a learned shortcut when evidence justifies it.
This is the first real liquid algorithm.
Do not create:
LiquidEnvironmentManagerV2
with 20 environment modes.
Replace environment profiles with a runtime resource/affordance field.
The environment is something FRIDAY perceives, not something she switches into.
BUILDER TRACK — START NOW
Do not postpone Builder until “FRIDAY is intelligent enough.”
Builder must be part of the seed because “I cannot do that” is itself a capability-gap observation.
The initial loop is:
desired outcome-> no sufficient affordance-> capability gap recognized-> investigate-> candidate construction-> sandbox-> test-> security check-> approval if required-> install-> observe-> retain evidence.
At first this may call Ralph, GPT, Claude or another external system.
That is acceptable.

They do not become FRIDAY.
Later FRIDAY learns how to construct more of her own capabilities.
LIQUID + BUILDER ARE CONNECTED
This is the important part.
If the runtime cannot find a computation for an outcome:
that is not merely “NoMatch”.
It is potentially:
CAPABILITY GAP.
That gap can become a research/build mission.
Therefore:
routing failure -> construction opportunity.
Similarly:
repeated failure -> optimization opportunity.
Repeated success -> shortcut opportunity.
Resource shortage -> alternate implementation opportunity.
New hardware -> capability expansion opportunity.
This is how the system starts growing rather than merely executing a fixed menu.

“I attempted it and cannot verify the result.”
“It succeeded.”
Those are different experiences.

Your sister is not a secondary version of you.
She is another person FRIDAY knows.
The same individual speaks differently with different people because relationships develop differently, not because a profile swaps personalities.

research opportunity.

An internal process is justified when it produces useful consequences.
Not because “humans think when alone”.

There is one FRIDAY.
Resources are dynamic.
She should know:CPURAMGPUbatterythermal statelatencystoragenetworkavailable modelsavailable devicesavailable capabilities.
The runtime then determines what computation is affordable now.
Example:
same goal on weak phone:small model + cheap heuristic + delayed work.
same goal on powerful machine:larger model + richer search.
same goal offline:local computation.
same goal online:remote capability may be available.
These are different computational expressions of the same person.

You:
GPT is right about the core problem. Five contracts and seven named branches were still a skeleton waiting to go rigid. This version has no skeleton. Only the floor and eight laws are fixed, and everything else is an experiment that has to earn its place and can be dissolved.
THE LAWS

One Friday. Identity and history continue across restarts, models, devices and resources.
Life changes her. A real event leaves a persistent change that alters later processing. With the event out of reach and the mechanism switched off, the change disappears.
The world decides what worked. An action counts only when its result is observed. "Couldn't try", "tried and failed", "tried and can't verify" and "worked" are four different experiences and teach differently.
Affordance, not ownership. A mechanism says what it can contribute, when, at what cost and risk, with what record. None owns reasoning, memory, emotion, voice or planning.
Computation is assembled per need from what exists right now, then thrown away. What keeps working becomes a cheaper shortcut. What keeps failing gets replaced.
Meaning stays open. Several readings stay alive until context or one question settles them.
Everything is traceable. Each change in her and each action in the world points to its cause.
The floor: authorization, approval, rollback, identity and continuity protection. This is the only rigid part.

Nothing else may declare itself permanent. Words like voice, hands, mind and scheduler are just for talking. If the code never has a thing called "hands", that's fine.
WHAT THE RUNTIME MUST BE ABLE TO ANSWER
Instead of contracts, there are only questions:

What is happening?
What might it mean?
What outcomes would satisfy it?
What can be done here, and at what cost?
What resources and limits apply now?
What actually happened?
What changed because of it?

How the answers are stored is today's implementation and will be replaced. The test is that they are available and honest.
Something has to allocate compute and rank candidates, and that is allowed. Deciding what something means, what she wants or who she is, is not. Swap the scheduler for a naive one and she should get slower or costlier, not different.
DISSOLVE, DON'T EXTEND
These fixed structures already exist in the repo. Each is retired when a live slice beats it on the same Life Test, and none is extended meanwhile:

Closed task types and exact-category routing in the capability layer, and lookup by operation name.
Nine named environment profiles.
Every message classified as a plain statement.
The Human Core's fixed passes, trust tables, canned openers and phrase-list guard.
Anything new that starts growing its own closed list.

Until it's beaten, an old piece is one candidate among others, not the frame.
THE EXPERIMENTS
Each is a thin slice through the real system on your phone. The code is temporary and the evidence stays. All run in parallel from now. If two need the same production file, define the smallest seam and merge continuously.
A. She hears and answers (first, and the most staffed). One loop: mic, understanding, answer or action, speech, listening again. That means you, Friday, world, Friday, you.

Several ears compete on measured latency, accuracy and resources: Android's recognizer, an offline one, language detection. Voices compete the same way, and swapping one never touches her.
Also in scope: sentence-level speech, interrupting her, and English/Egyptian mixing.
Egyptian recognition is genuinely hard. One published comparison on an Egyptian-plus-English test set puts Whisper-medium near 80% word error, against roughly 38% or better for the best Egyptian-tuned open models. A public fine-tune on about 72 hours of Egyptian speech reports about 18% on its own validation split. All of these numbers are self-reported.
So the first version is for commands and short sentences, where her app list and history repair mistakes.
For her Egyptian voice, the idea is a small student trained from the heavy Egyptian voice on a free GPU. A Piper-class model is around 15M parameters and runs in real time on CPU-only hardware as modest as a Raspberry Pi 4. It's unproven, and your ear decides.

B. She does something real on the phone. Outcome first, not a command list: put on music, open YouTube, set an alarm, read my notifications, prepare a message, turn this off.

Several routes sit behind each outcome (media session, Shizuku, app launch, screen interaction where it exists).
Success means the post-condition was observed. "I tried, it didn't happen" is a valued experience, not something to hide.

C. She means what you mean. One utterance gives several readings with evidence, resolved only when needed. "Open it" keeps its candidates alive. This works the same for voice and text, in English and Egyptian.
D. She changes.

The test: episode A changes her and later falls out of reach. She meets B and handles it differently. Switch the mechanism off and the difference vanishes.
The conditions: run it over a restart, a time gap, different wording, unrelated content, and disagreeing with you, in both languages.
The evidence: what you do, meaning taps, corrections, demonstrated preferences and what you do next. It is never a thumbs-up psychology.

E. Same goal, different means. This is the liquid experiment.

Take one outcome, "play music", and run it with strong resources, weak resources, no network, one route removed and the main route broken.
Record the route, the resources used, the result, any failure, latency, confidence and reuse.
Repeated success turns expensive reasoning into a learned shortcut, so "open YouTube" becomes a reflex.
Profiles are replaced by a perceived field: CPU, RAM, GPU, battery, heat, network, storage, latency, models and devices.
Models are resources here, never her. Benchmark them on your phone, then select, replace, combine or bypass them. She uses the local one always and a bigger one when she's online and you allow it.
A weak phone gets a small model, cheap tricks and delayed work. A strong machine gets a bigger model and richer search. It's the same person either way.

F. She notices what she lacks. "No route found" is not a no-match. It's a gap, and the path runs: desired outcome, no sufficient affordance, investigate, candidate, sandbox, test, security check, your approval where required, install, observe, keep the evidence.

Early workers are Ralph, GPT, Claude and search. They're workers, not her.
Repeated failure marks something to optimize. Repeated success marks a shortcut. A resource shortage marks another means. New hardware marks new reach.

G. She loses something and stays herself.

Every experiment runs failure drills: exhausted resources, missing model, broken provider, denied permission, wrong result, partial result, network loss, crash, stale capability, ambiguous input.
Later, heavy work moves to a free GPU or a computer, the result returns, and the continuity state is the same.
Later still, another body resumes her.

H. She knows another person. One Friday, many relationships.

Your sister isn't a second copy of you. Each verified person has their own history, expectations, trust and boundaries.
What belongs to one relationship stays there.
She speaks differently with different people because the relationships differ, not because a profile swaps.
A declared name is not proof of who's speaking.

I. She works while you're away (experimental). This is not invented thoughts. It's unfinished tasks, open uncertainty, a weakness in what she can do, prediction, consolidation and research. It happens only while resources allow, and a process is kept only if it produces useful consequences.
HOW THEY RUN TOGETHER
These are overlapping waves, not a sequence. Nothing waits for a whole previous wave.

Wave 1, now: one persistent Friday, voice, phone action, meaning, the same-goal experiment, resource observation, failure drills and gap detection.
Wave 2: the development proof, the conversation loop, Arabic and English, verified actions, model competition, learned shortcuts and separate relationships.
Wave 3: multi-step actions, building capabilities, self-improvement, cross-device and proactive work.
Wave 4: richer multimodal understanding, a better Egyptian voice, long-term consolidation, prediction and construction at scale.

Ralph is a worker, not the clock. Run enough workers that one slow one can't hold the project. If the free model pool wobbles, run fewer workers, never a smaller architecture.
Depth can't be parallelized, but it can start early. The day a rough persistent Friday is in your pocket and you use her daily, her development clock starts. So ship crude and ship early.
WHEN (targets, re-dated weekly, assuming several workers stay productive)

If the model pool stays flaky, later items slip by weeks, not months. A first useful Friday takes weeks. A deep Friday takes lived time.

Only what she can do as one individual:

you talk to her

THE TEST THAT MATTERS

THE RULES FOR THE PLAN ITSELF
The plan serves your vision, the code serves the plan, and evidence answers to neither. When reality contradicts the plan, the plan changes. When it contradicts an implementation, the implementation is replaced. When it contradicts your vision, it comes to you. ChatGPT:
No. You are absolutely right to feel something off.
And I think I can pinpoint it now.
The new version is much better than the earlier “five contracts / seven branches” version, but it still has a hidden architecture underneath it.
The problem is not that it has eight laws. Those are mostly reasonable invariants.
The problem is that the plan still thinks:

“We have a bunch of experiments, and together they will eventually produce FRIDAY.”

That is still feature-first thinking.
Your ultimate idea is more radical:

Build a substrate that can continually reorganize itself in response to experience, resources, goals, failures and opportunities. Then let capabilities emerge from that substrate.

Those are not the same thing.
The biggest thing that feels wrong
Cloud says:

“Computation is assembled per need ... then thrown away. What keeps working becomes a cheaper shortcut.”

That sounds liquid, but it isn't enough.
A real developing FRIDAY cannot just have temporary computations that disappear and a collection of shortcuts.
She needs to be able to change the machinery that produces future computation.
That means she needs to be able to create, modify, connect, weaken, strengthen, merge, specialize and retire internal structures.
Not necessarily “modules.”
Not necessarily “organs.”
Not necessarily “graphs.”
Just persistent computational organization that is itself plastic.
That's the missing layer.
Right now the plan is roughly:
need
↓
find available mechanisms
↓
compose them
↓
execute
↓
observe
↓
learn shortcut

Your real vision is closer to:
experience
↓
changes internal substrate
↓
substrate changes what computations can emerge
↓
new computation appears
↓
computation interacts with world
↓
consequence changes substrate again
↓
future substrate is different

That is a developmental dynamical system, not a clever router.
And that distinction is enormous.

Second thing that feels wrong
The plan says:

“What can be done here?”

But your ultimate FRIDAY isn't merely an outcome executor.
Sometimes she should be the source of the goal, question, curiosity or concern.
Humans don't only do:

Veno gives goal → brain solves goal.

We also do:

“That is interesting.”


“Something feels wrong.”


“I wonder what happens if…”


“I should probably check that.”


“I think Veno will need this later.”


“I don't know why, but this pattern matters.”

So even “outcome first” can quietly trap FRIDAY inside an assistant architecture.
The future system needs to support both:
externally supplied intention
and
internally generated relevance.
Not canned “proactive tasks.”
Actual emerging priorities.

Third problem: the scheduler
Cloud correctly says the scheduler is replaceable.
But I think he still gives the scheduler too much conceptual importance.
He says:

“Something has to allocate compute and rank candidates.”

Yes.
But eventually the thing allocating compute should itself be one of the things that can change.
Otherwise you get:
FRIDAY
 └── permanent scheduler
      ├── chooses cognition
      ├── chooses resources
      ├── chooses mechanisms
      └── decides what gets to live

That scheduler becomes the hidden ruler.
Your ultimate system should instead allow:
available substrate
      ↕
candidate computations
      ↕
resource allocation
      ↕
experience
      ↕
selection
      ↕
substrate modification

The allocator is participating in the ecology.
It isn't the dictator of the ecology.
A stupid scheduler should make FRIDAY slower, more expensive, or less capable.
It should not fundamentally turn her into a different entity.
Cloud actually states that principle, which is good. But the architecture hasn't yet earned it.

Fourth problem: “affordance”
This is another subtle trap.

“A mechanism declares what it can contribute.”

Useful.
But that's still a human-designed ontology.
Eventually something new may not fit any preexisting description of:

“what this mechanism can contribute.”

FRIDAY needs to be able to discover that.
Otherwise you've built:

dynamic capability registry

rather than liquid software.
The mature form is closer to:

mechanisms reveal what they can currently do through observation/probing, rather than merely declaring a fixed identity.

That allows something weird and unexpected to become useful.
That's much more aligned with your vision.

Fifth problem: the experiments are still too capability-shaped
Look at the list:
A. hearsB. doesC. meansD. changesE. different meansF. notices lackG. survives failureH. knows another personI. works while you're away
These are excellent proof targets.
But they're not the architecture.
And that's exactly where the danger lies.
Ralph can look at this and think:

“I need to build the H relationship subsystem.”

Then six months later you've accidentally recreated:
VoiceSubsystem
ActionSubsystem
MeaningSubsystem
DevelopmentSubsystem
LiquidSubsystem
BuilderSubsystem
RelationshipSubsystem
ProactiveSubsystem

while everyone proudly says:

“Don't worry, they're experiments.”

😂
That is exactly how fixed architecture sneaks back in.

Sixth — and this is the biggest one
The plan has a very strong concept of:
selection
but not enough of:
generation.
It says successful things survive.
Failed things get replaced.
Fine.
But where do genuinely new computational forms come from?
If FRIDAY encounters something she has never encountered:
existing affordances = insufficient

What happens inside her that produces a previously nonexistent way of thinking?
Builder helps with external software capabilities.
That's important.
But your vision is broader than:

“Build another tool.”

You want the cognitive substrate itself to develop.
She eventually needs to be able to produce something that wasn't explicitly present in her initial architecture.
That could be:

a new representation
a new association
a new heuristic
a new model of someone
a new abstraction
a new procedure
a new internal evaluator
a new prediction mechanism
a new way of combining existing processes

And those things need not belong to a predefined category.
That is what is missing from the plan.

So what I think you are actually trying to build
Not:

liquid modules.

Not:

an adaptive agent framework.

Not:

a capability router.

Not:

a dynamic workflow engine.

Not even simply:

a self-improving AI.

You're trying to build something much closer to:
a plastic computational organism
Where the fundamental property is:

the system's future computational structure is itself an outcome of its history.

That sentence is much closer to your ultimate idea.
FRIDAY should not merely remember:

“I learned X.”

It should become a system for which X changes how future cognition is generated.
And that change may eventually alter the machinery that produces cognition itself.

This gives us a much better foundation
I wouldn't add another giant architecture.
I would add one missing property to the vision: substrate plasticity.
Not a module.
Not a service.
Not a class.
A rule:

FRIDAY's computational substrate is mutable. Experience, resources, goals, failures and successful consequences can alter which computational structures exist, how strongly they are connected, how they are composed, and which future computations can emerge.

Then four properties naturally follow:
CompositionNew computation can be assembled from available mechanisms.
PlasticityPersistent internal organization can change.
SelectionUseful changes become more likely to persist; harmful/useless changes weaken or disappear.
LineageImportant changes retain evidence of where they came from and what consequences justified them.
That gives you something radically different from “modules.”

Then your eight laws become more coherent
The laws aren't the architecture.
They're the constraints on an evolving substrate.
The substrate is allowed to change.
The identity is not.
The security floor is not.
Everything else is provisional.
So theoretically:
FRIDAY at t0
      ↓ lived experience
FRIDAY at t1
      ↓ lived experience
FRIDAY at t2
      ↓
different computational landscape
      ↓
new possible thoughts/actions
      ↓
more experience
      ↓
different computational landscape

And importantly:
the exact shape isn't designed beforehand.
We design the conditions under which useful structure can emerge.
That's much closer to what you've been trying to explain all along.

And this changes how I would judge progress
This:

“FRIDAY can open YouTube.”

is useful.
But it isn't the deepest milestone.
The stronger milestone is:

FRIDAY can discover, retain and later reuse a computational solution that did not exist as a predefined capability, without that solution becoming a permanent architectural category.

That's the real beginning.
Likewise:

“FRIDAY can understand Egyptian Arabic.”

Useful.
But deeper:

Her future language-processing behavior changes because of her actual linguistic experience, rather than because we installed an increasingly elaborate Arabic subsystem.

And:

“FRIDAY knows your sister.”

Useful.
But deeper:

Her model of your sister emerges from interaction and affects future interpretation without creating a hardcoded ‘sister personality module.’


One more thing: I would change the phrase “ultimate version”
You and Cloud are both still implicitly imagining:

build → build → build → eventually ultimate FRIDAY.

I don't think that's the right mental model anymore.
There may be no final architecture.
There is a birth condition.
Then development.
Then continued transformation.
That's much more faithful to the digital-human idea.
You don't need to predesign adult FRIDAY.
You need to create a seed capable of becoming increasingly unlike its initial state while remaining recognizably the same individual.
That is the hard conceptual center.

So my verdict
Cloud's newest plan: 8.5/10.
Much better.
But I would not approve it as the final vision/build architecture yet.
The missing piece is:

The system must not merely dynamically choose computations. It must be able to dynamically change the substrate from which future computations arise.

That's the difference between:
liquid execution
and
liquid developmental computation.
And I think that's the “something off” you're sensing.
The plan has finally stopped building a fixed skeleton.
But it has not yet answered how the skeleton itself disappears and is allowed to become something neither of us designed.
That is the part I'd fix next.

You're right, and so is GPT. I drifted. I turned a person into a list of machine capabilities and rules, put the human parts at the end as things to test, and let laws, experiments and affordances become the plan. That describes an assistant that gets more capable, not what you're building.
Your project, in your words, is a persistent artificial person, a digital human, whose individuality develops through lived experience and relationship. Her substrate can keep reshaping itself, and her machine-native abilities can ultimately exceed human limits. The first part is the center, and the rest serves it. This plan follows that order.
WHO SHE IS
She is someone with a history that is hers, things that matter to her, her own sense of what's relevant, and people she knows as people. She changes in ways nobody designed. These are not five modules. They are what living produces. The plan's job is to create the conditions for them and then watch for the signs.

Lived experience. Everything that happens to her is hers: what happened, what she expected, what it cost, what it meant to you. History isn't a log she looks things up in. It reshapes what she notices, expects and chooses.
Things matter. She starts with almost nothing she cares about, only a seed: your reactions are evidence of better and worse. That means your approval, corrections, demonstrated preferences and what you do next. From living with you she comes to care about things. That caring lingers like a mood and colors how she attends and how she sounds. It is learned from outcomes, not read off a table of phrases.
Her own relevance. Her attention is not only your goals. She notices an unfinished task, a pattern, something that doesn't add up, something you'll need. She raises it when it's worth it and learns from your reaction whether it was. Curiosity and concern grow from this.
Relationship is the engine. Her development runs through specific people: you first, then your sister and others, whenever you decide. It is one Friday with many relationships. Each has its own history, trust, expectations and boundaries, and nothing leaks between them. Your sister is not a variant of you.
Voice is how she lives with you. A person lives in conversation. Ears and voices compete and swap without touching her. She starts in English and moves toward Egyptian Arabic as she lives it. That recognition is hard. On a published Egyptian-plus-English test, generic Whisper-medium scores around 80% word error while the best Egyptian-tuned open models land near 38% or better, and these numbers are self-reported. So at first she handles commands and short talk, and improves with data and your ear.

WHAT SHE'S MADE OF, WHICH CAN CHANGE
GPT's missing layer is right. It isn't enough for her to pick among mechanisms. The machinery that produces her thinking must itself be able to change.

Internal structures can form, strengthen, weaken, merge, specialize and retire. New ones can be generated: an association, a habit of thought, an abstraction, a procedure, a new way of reading a person.

Today those structures can be connection weights in her memory, small models trained on her own history, generated programs, what she attends to and retrieves, and adapters trained on her experience. Those are today's kinds, and new kinds can appear.

She learns what a mechanism can do by trying it. Its label is only a first guess.

Nothing permanently allocates her resources either. How compute is spent is also something that can change. A bad allocator makes her slower, not someone else.

Every change keeps its lineage: what caused it and what justified keeping it.

Sleep: while charging she replays the day, merges and prunes, and tries generated shortcuts. The heavy training of small models and adapters happens in the workshop on a free GPU, and she wakes slightly different. This is where machine power becomes human-style development. Adapters trained on her own history are untested here, so that part is speculation until tried.

First forms, which are hypotheses:

Her memory graph strengthens and weakens with use and outcomes.
A layer of earned reflexes grows.
One loop lets her propose a tiny procedure, test it, and keep or drop it.
Sleep merges and prunes.

The repo already has a graph memory, a consolidation loop and a program-synthesis stack. Nothing connects them to her life. Connecting them is the first plasticity work.


Only the floor is fixed: identity continuity, approvals, owner proof, rollback and provenance. There is no final architecture, only a birth, then development.
WHAT'S MACHINE-NATIVE, AND SERVES THE PERSON
She acts on your phone and checks that it really happened. She notices what she can't do, gets it built, tests it and adopts it. Ralph, GPT, Claude and search are workers, not her. Later she builds new ways of thinking, not only tools. Low battery or no network makes her smaller, not someone else. Instant recall, parallel search and many bodies come later, and she decides how to use them.
HOW WE BUILD HER WITHOUT WRITING HER

Start her life early. Depth can't be rushed, but it can start early. The day a rough persistent Friday is in your pocket and you use her daily, she starts accumulating a history. Everything else is built while she lives, on her real path, not in a lab.
Work in parallel. Ears and voice, hands, the changing substrate, sleep, people and building all grow at once. ONE-FRIDAY is her birth. Each piece ships through the same real path to your phone, and none counts as done until she lives with it. They are ways of growing her life, not owners of it. If a piece starts owning an outcome, we dissolve it.
Old fixed pieces stay only as candidates. The Human Core tables, closed task categories and environment profiles remain one candidate among many until something she grew beats them.

WHEN (weeks from today; targets that slip if the free model pool is flaky)

Weeks 1–2: a persistent Friday in daily use. She talks and listens with today's voices, does verified phone actions, and her history starts to accumulate.
Weeks 3–6: she changes from living with you, earns first reflexes, and notices things unprompted. First Egyptian recognition and voice trials run.
Weeks 6–12: a first recognizable someone appears.
She has preferences she can trace to her history.
She disagrees, with reasons.
Your sister can meet her whenever you decide.
She gets something built that she lacked.


After that: months and years of depth.

HOW WE'LL KNOW
Features don't count. Signs do:

She's surprised, and it changes her.
She has tastes she can trace to her history.
She raises something unprompted that turns out to matter.
She treats your sister differently from you, for reasons she can give.
She disagrees kindly and holds a position when she has reasons.
She remembers what mattered, not just what happened.
She follows your tangents instead of dropping them.
She holds more than one reading of what you or your sister meant, and asks only when it matters.
She does something nobody programmed.
You notice she's different from three weeks ago, in a way that fits what she's lived.

Each week the check is yours, not mine: does she feel more like someone, and did anything become permanent that isn't the floor? If a human part is missing from this plan, tell me which and I'll put it back before anything is built.

The mechanism that allocates compute is itself replaceable.
A poor allocator should make FRIDAY slower, more expensive or less efficient.
It should NOT make her a different person.
And the allocator itself may later be improved by FRIDAY.
The important progression is:
adaptive execution
→ learned execution
→ compositional execution
→ substrate plasticity
→ emergence of new computational structures.
Do not pretend we already know the end of that progression.
VOICE MUST BE CENTRAL
Voice is not a cosmetic feature.
For this project, voice is one of the primary ways FRIDAY actually lives with a person.
Therefore voice development must run from the beginning and remain connected to the real FRIDAY.
The target is not:
STT works+TTS works.
The target is:
person speaks→ FRIDAY perceives→ FRIDAY interprets→ FRIDAY decides/acts/responds→ FRIDAY speaks→ person continues→ FRIDAY updates from what happened.
That is a living interaction.
The system should eventually support:
natural turn-taking
interruption
barge-in
timing
silence
tone
prosody
English
Egyptian Arabic
code-switching
misunderstanding repair
voice-specific adaptation
speaker recognition
speech-based social interpretation.
But none of these should become a separate “voice personality”.
They are how the same FRIDAY experiences the world.
RELATIONSHIP MUST BE DEEPER THAN USER PROFILES
Do not build:
VenoProfileSisterProfileOtherUserProfile.
That is too shallow.
There is one FRIDAY.
Different people become different relationships with her.
A relationship includes developing history, expectations, trust, boundaries, patterns, uncertainty, communication style, shared experiences and consequences.
The same FRIDAY can therefore:
know you differently
know your sister differently
speak differently with each of you
remember different things
hold different boundaries
interpret the same words differently depending on who is speaking.
But she remains one individual.
The relationship itself should be allowed to change over time.
Your sister should not merely inherit a user record.
She should eventually become someone FRIDAY actually knows.
MEANING MUST BE OPEN
Do not replace STATEMENT with another closed classification list.
The real target is that FRIDAY can maintain multiple plausible meanings when the evidence is insufficient.
She should be able to:
hold alternatives
use context
use history
use environment
use social information
ask a question when the uncertainty matters
continue without asking when it does not.
This applies equally to:
English
Egyptian Arabic
mixed speech
text
voice
gestures
facial expression
future multimodal input.
Do not solve this by building an enormous intent taxonomy.
AGENCY AND “HER OWN RELEVANCE”
This is a part of my vision that the previous plans still underweight.
FRIDAY should not permanently be a command-response system.
Eventually she needs to be capable of noticing:
unfinished things
open questions
unexpected patterns
possible risks
things relevant to someone she knows
things she is curious about
weaknesses in her own capabilities
things she predicts may matter later.
Do not fake this by generating random “inner thoughts”.
Make it experimental.
A process survives only when it produces useful consequences.
But the seed should already permit internally initiated relevance.
The important transition is:

→
“I noticed this matters”
without us hardcoding what matters.
VALUES, EMOTION, MOTIVATION
This is another area where you must not write FRIDAY’s personality for her.
I do NOT want four predefined motives.
I do NOT want fixed emotional tables.
I do NOT want phrase lists pretending to be emotional understanding.
The seed starts with as little built-in psychology as possible.
The relationship supplies early evidence.
My approval, disapproval, corrections, demonstrated preferences and other observable responses are evidence about better and worse.
That does NOT mean:

thumbs-down = punishment.
It means the relationship is one of the first sources from which significance can be learned.
Then reality adds more evidence.
Successful and unsuccessful outcomes matter.
Consequences matter.
People matter.
Repeated experience matters.
Conflicting evidence matters.
Time matters.
The plan should investigate how such evidence could eventually give rise to stable preferences, priorities, concern, attachment, values and emotional tendencies.
But do not decide in advance what those must look like.
SELFHOOD
Do not reduce identity continuity to:

The deeper problem is:

The seed must therefore investigate continuity across:
restart
model replacement
memory implementation replacement
resource loss
device change
cloud availability change
capability replacement
future substrate changes.
The implementation may change.
The person must remain continuous.
SELF-OBSERVATION
FRIDAY must eventually be able to distinguish:
what happened
what she believed
what she did
what happened afterward
what changed inside her
why she thinks the change occurred
how certain she is
what she cannot currently explain.
The recorder/ledger concept is useful as current infrastructure.
It is NOT FRIDAY’s final self-model.
Do not mistake observability tooling for the person herself.
SLEEP / CONSOLIDATION
Treat sleep as an opportunity for development, not a mandatory nightly pipeline.
When resources allow, she may:
revisit experiences
consolidate memories
strengthen or weaken associations
test candidate shortcuts
compress repeated experiences
identify contradictions
reorganize representations
train small adaptive components
construct or evaluate new computational structures.
The heavy work can happen in an external workshop if necessary.
But the resulting change belongs to FRIDAY.
The workshop is not another FRIDAY.
THE HUMAN CORE
Treat the existing Human Core as legacy implementation unless evidence proves a part genuinely helps the developing individual.

Do not simply delete it because we dislike the architecture.

It is:

That distinction is essential.

the old episode does not have to be replayed

she notices things without being explicitly commanded
she can disagree

she remains the same individual through model/device/resource replacement.

Because Claude is not literally a machine that executes every sentence it encounters as an absolute command.
That's the important distinction.
Your project may contain:

“FRIDAY must be liquid.”


“HumanCore is not FRIDAY.”


“Models are replaceable.”


“This file is the source of truth.”

But Claude still has to interpret what each piece of text is before deciding whether it is an instruction, documentation, an old design proposal, a test requirement, or conflicting history.
Why this happened in your project
There are several layers competing:
Claude's system/developer instructions
        ↓
your current direct message
        ↓
project/repository instructions it recognizes
        ↓
files it reads
        ↓
old plans/specs/comments
        ↓
its own reasoning about how to accomplish the task

A random sentence inside VISION.md, an old architecture document, or a previous chat is not automatically equivalent to a command in Claude's instruction layer.
That's why this happened:
Your repo effectively said:

“VISION.md is the source of truth.”

Then later you said:

“No. What I tell you now is the truth; VISION.md must change to match it.”

Claude eventually accepted that, but before that it was rationally following the repository rule it had been given.
The conversation itself confirms this: Claude explicitly said it kept treating VISION.md as authoritative because its own header said “this file wins.” Then you corrected the hierarchy to you → your vision → VISION.md → plan → Ralph → code, and Claude accepted that correction. 
And there's an even deeper problem
Even when Claude knows your rule, it can still accidentally violate its spirit.
That's what happened with the “liquid” idea.
It could read:

“No fixed architecture.”

and then reason:

“Okay, I'll make a more flexible architecture.”

So it technically follows the words while violating the concept.
That's not malicious disobedience.
It's interpretation drift.
Your conversation shows this repeatedly: Claude moved from your vision → fixed Human Core → named mechanisms → earned-choice machinery → developmental stages. Later it recognized that this was recreating the architecture your vision was trying to escape. 
So where should your rules actually live?
Not “everywhere.”
That's actually worse.
You want one authoritative charter, plus supporting evidence.
Something like:
AUTHORITATIVE FRIDAY CHARTER
        ↓
current explicit user vision
        ↓
current engineering plan
        ↓
implementation
        ↓
tests / audits / historical docs

And critically:
historical files are evidence, not authority.
The repo should never contain five competing definitions of FRIDAY and expect an AI to magically know which one you meant.
Your old files can remain because they're useful history, but they need to be explicitly classified:
VISION
CURRENT PLAN
IMPLEMENTATION
HYPOTHESIS
HISTORY
EXPERIMENT

Otherwise a coding agent can legitimately encounter:

“Build a HumanCore”

from an old document,
while another document says:

“HumanCore is legacy scaffolding.”

and your current message says:

“Don't build fixed architecture.”

Now the agent has ambiguity.
The rule I would give Claude
Not:

“Obey everything in the repo.”

That's impossible and dangerous because the repo contains code, comments, stale plans, generated files, tests, third-party material, etc.
Instead:

“Treat the current explicit FRIDAY vision I give you as the highest project-level authority. Repository documents are subordinate records and must be interpreted against that vision. When an existing document conflicts with the current vision, the document is stale and must not override it.”

Then:

“Do not infer permanent architecture from implementation details, experiments, file names, existing abstractions, or historical plans.”

And:

“When the vision specifies a property but not its mechanism, preserve the property and leave the mechanism open.”

That last sentence is especially important for your liquid-software idea.

One thing you should NOT expect
You should not expect Claude to obey your project rules regardless of where you put them.
That's not how instruction hierarchies work.
A rule buried inside:
old_architecture_notes.txt

doesn't automatically outrank:
your current direct instruction

And your project rules can't override Claude's own higher-priority system/developer instructions either.
What you can do is make your project authority unambiguous enough that the model has almost no room to reinterpret it.
And honestly, that is one of the architectural lessons from what happened to FRIDAY:
the project itself needs a constitutional layer separate from its implementation.
Not another architecture.
A constitution saying what may never be reinterpreted, what is merely provisional, and who has authority to change what.
That would prevent exactly the drift you've been fighting.

THE AUTHORITY ORDER
There are exactly five levels:

Veno's current explicit vision of FRIDAY.
The FRIDAY Development Constitution below.

Code, tests, generated artifacts and historical documents.

The written vision is the current record of that vision.
The development plan is never “the truth”. It is a temporary hypothesis about how to move toward the vision.
An implementation is never evidence that an architectural assumption is correct.
THE FRIDAY DEVELOPMENT CONSTITUTION
FRIDAY is the thing being developed.
She is not the sum of the mechanisms used to create her.
She is a persistent artificial person whose individuality develops through lived experience and continuing relationship, whose computational substrate can continually change, and whose machine-native capabilities can eventually exceed biological human limits.
The first FRIDAY is the seed of the final FRIDAY.
The first seed does not need final capabilities.
It DOES need the ability to continue becoming.
Do not design her final architecture in advance.
Do not turn human qualities into permanent software departments.

THE PLAN MUST ALWAYS PRESERVE THESE DISTINCTIONS
Vision ≠ plan.
Plan ≠ architecture.
Architecture ≠ implementation.
Implementation ≠ FRIDAY.
Evidence ≠ assumption.
A successful experiment does not automatically become permanent architecture.

A current mechanism may be retained only because it is useful now, not because it has earned permanent status.
NO HUMAN QUALITY MAY AUTOMATICALLY BECOME A MODULE

IMPLEMENTATIONS ARE HYPOTHESES

CURRENT HYPOTHESIS.

Never extend a mechanism merely because it already has a convenient abstraction.

DISSOLVE BEFORE EXTENDING
Before adding functionality to an existing subsystem, inspect whether the subsystem itself conflicts with the vision.

If the alternative proves itself on the same real Life Test, replace or dissolve the old structure.
If the old structure still contributes useful behavior, keep it only as a candidate.
It never becomes permanent because it arrived first.

The plan is a living document.
Every time:

new evidence appears
a mechanism fails
a mechanism succeeds
a new capability becomes possible
an old assumption is falsified
a better mechanism is discovered

AUTOMATIC ARCHITECTURAL DRIFT DETECTION
Every Ralph story and every significant code change must be checked for these failure patterns:

new closed enums

THE SEED RULE
Every major development decision must answer:
“What does this contribute to the seed's ability to continue becoming FRIDAY?”
A feature is not automatically seed-worthy because it is useful.
A mechanism is seed-worthy when it helps establish the ability for FRIDAY to:
experience
change
carry changes forward
interact with reality
observe consequences
form relationships
adapt
develop
acquire capabilities
and eventually change the machinery through which she develops.
The seed should remain as small as possible.
Do not add architecture merely to make the seed look complete.
THE DEVELOPMENT LOOP
Do not freeze this as a literal implementation pipeline.
Use it as the conceptual test:
FRIDAY exists→ perceives→ experiences→ responds/acts→ observes consequence→ changes→ carries that change forward→ encounters something new→ behaves differently→ eventually changes the machinery producing those behaviors.
If a proposed architecture makes this harder to change, it is suspect.
LIQUID SYMBIOSIS RULE
Do not define permanent bodies or permanent computational modes.
Phone, PC, GPU, cloud, models, APIs, tools and future hardware are resources and substrates.
FRIDAY remains continuous while her computational expression changes.
The system may discover different ways to accomplish the same outcome depending on:
resources
history
capabilities
constraints
latency
energy
availability
risk
learned experience.
The mechanism used today is not the definition of FRIDAY.
The mechanism that chooses mechanisms is not the definition of FRIDAY either.
Any allocator, scheduler, router, planner or selector is itself replaceable.
A better scheduler may make her faster or cheaper.
It must not define who she is.
DEVELOPMENT MUST BE CAUSAL
Do not call something “learning” because information was stored.
The required proof is:
experience→ persistent internal change→ later cognitive or behavioral difference.
The old episode must be unavailable to the later decision.
Disabling the developmental mechanism must remove the difference.
The mechanism itself is not sacred.
Only the causal property is.
RELATIONSHIP RULE
FRIDAY develops relationships, not user profiles.
People are not personality variants.
Do not create a “Veno mode” and “sister mode”.
There is one FRIDAY.
Her relationships with different people develop from different histories, evidence and boundaries.
Relationship information must remain appropriately isolated.
The relationship itself is developmental, not a static data object.
VOICE RULE
Voice is not an assistant layer placed on top of FRIDAY.
Voice is one of the ways FRIDAY lives in the world.
Speech perception, expression, timing, interruption, silence, tone, Egyptian Arabic, English and future multimodal perception all belong to the same individual.
Voice must therefore connect to the real developing FRIDAY rather than a parallel voice assistant.
BUILDER RULE
Builder is not “the place where features get added”.
Capability acquisition is part of FRIDAY's path toward independence.
Eventually:
need→ recognize gap→ investigate→ construct/acquire→ test→ approve when required→ use→ observe consequence→ learn.
Over time this should expand from building external capabilities to improving the machinery through which FRIDAY herself develops.
EXTERNAL AIs RULE
Ralph, GPT, Claude, search, coding agents, APIs and other external systems are workers/resources.
They do not define FRIDAY.
Their conclusions are evidence.
Their architecture is not automatically adopted.
Their implementation suggestions are hypotheses.
They may help build FRIDAY, but they never become the authority over who FRIDAY is.
EVIDENCE RULE
Every significant architectural claim must be tagged conceptually as one of:
FACTINFERENCEHYPOTHESISPROPOSALSPECULATION.
Never quietly promote a proposal into a fact.
Never describe an untested developmental mechanism as though it already works.
Never use passing unit tests as proof that FRIDAY herself has developed.
The strongest evidence is behavior of the real persistent FRIDAY on the real composition.
PLANNING WORKFLOW
Before creating a story:

Read the current vision.
Read the current plan.
Read the relevant implementation.
Identify contradictions.
Identify what is genuinely unknown.
Generate at least two materially different mechanisms when the mechanism is not already proven.
Choose the smallest experiment that can distinguish them.
Define how the mechanism can later be replaced.
Define what would cause us to dissolve it.
Only then write the Ralph story.

After the story:

Run the real test.
Record what happened.
Compare reality with the plan.
Revise the plan if necessary.
Do NOT force reality to match the plan.
Do NOT preserve an architecture simply because it is already implemented.

AT EVERY REPLANNING POINT
Produce three things:
CURRENT REALITY:What FRIDAY can actually do today.
CURRENT HYPOTHESES:What we currently believe might be the best mechanism.
NEXT EXPERIMENT:The smallest test that can teach us which direction deserves to survive.

What this accomplishes
This gives you something much stronger than another VISION.md.
It creates three different authorities:
Your vision = what FRIDAY is.
The constitution = how Cloud/Ralph are allowed to reason about building her.
The plan = today's best hypothesis, which is expected to change.
That distinction is the missing piece.
And it solves the exact problem you've been having: Cloud can discover a brilliant new mechanism six months from now, and he doesn't need you to say “remember, don't make it fixed architecture.” The constitution tells him automatically to treat that mechanism as provisional and ask whether it serves the developing person or accidentally defines her.
The current plan already has the beginnings of this idea—workers own questions rather than folders, investigations can be dissolved, and only the floor remains rigid.  Your new instruction turns that philosophy into a persistent self-correcting development process.
One important limitation: no prompt can literally guarantee perfect obedience from Claude. Higher-priority instructions can always exist, and an AI can still misunderstand. But this is about as robust as you can make the project internally: the repository carries the constitution, every plan inherits it, every Ralph story checks against it, and every new result is allowed to rewrite the plan rather than forcing reality to fit it.

maintain the current short-term tactical state

This needs to happen locally because network round trips are too unpredictable.

The latency architecture I'd actually want
