ChatGPT:
Mate, YES. This is exactly the kind of ability FRIDAY should eventually develop: not just learning facts, but discovering new ways to perceive, infer, predict, and understand things that she couldn't previously understand.
You're not asking me to add bat sonar to FRIDAY. You're asking why she couldn't invent her own equivalent of sonar, develop House-like medical reasoning, or learn to make Jane-like observations.
And that's a much bigger question.
1. The real capability: discovering ways to know what you cannot directly observe

  
    
    **The bat**

It emits sound, analyzes returning echoes, and estimates objects' locations and distances.

  
  
    
    **The diagnostician**

Observes symptoms, forms competing explanations, and seeks evidence to distinguish them.

  
  
    
    **The scientist**

Develops a hypothesis, designs an experiment, measures what happens, and revises the explanation.

  
  
    
    **The artificial intelligence**

Combines sensors, physical models, learned patterns and experiments to infer properties of its environment.

  


These look like different abilities, but they share a deeper principle:
There is something hidden. Available evidence is incomplete. The system discovers a way to obtain or interpret more evidence, then uses it to infer what is happening.
That is the general capability worth pursuing in FRIDAY.
2. Your bat example: she could invent a new sense
<AsyncImageGroup query={["bat echolocation outgoing ultrasound echo returning distance diagram","smartphone speaker microphone acoustic ranging reflection research illustration"]} aspectRatio="5:4" layout="carousel" numPerQuery={1}/>
Imagine telling FRIDAY:

"Friday, figure out how to determine the distance between you and an object without using your camera."

A conventional assistant searches for an existing distance-measuring application.
A more capable FRIDAY would investigate how distance could be inferred at all.
She might discover acoustic ranging:

Emit a known sound.
Record the sound returning from an object.
Detect the time difference between transmission and reflection.
Account for the speed of sound and the device's own audio latency.
Calculate an estimated distance.
Compare that estimate against independently measured distances.
Investigate why the measurement fails under different conditions.

The basic relationship is:
[d \approx \frac{c\Delta t}{2}]
Here, (d) is the estimated distance, (c) is the speed of sound, and (\Delta t) is the round-trip travel time after accounting for system delays.
Bats use echo timing and additional acoustic information to estimate distance and locate objects. Journal of Experimental Biology
Now imagine something more interesting.
She tries acoustic ranging, discovers that her hardware cannot reliably separate nearby reflections, and investigates alternatives. Perhaps she combines audio with the camera, motion sensors, or another available signal.
The goal stays the same. The method changes as she learns what works.
One practical limitation: ordinary phone audio hardware may restrict ultrasonic sensing. Researchers demonstrated much stronger acoustic sensing after reconfiguring smartphone hardware and software, including experiments detecting human presence several metres away. Those results do not mean every stock Android phone can do the same thing. PowerPhone research
This is where your liquid-software idea becomes powerful. FRIDAY shouldn't assume she has a permanent list of senses. She should be able to discover what the current environment allows her to sense, develop a method, and retain it if it proves useful.
3. Now apply the same principle to Dr. House


You tell FRIDAY:

"I want you to develop the ability to identify medical conditions by observing symptoms, finding subtle connections, and making excellent predictions."

The naive implementation would download medical books, install a medical model, and declare the feature complete.
That's not what you're aiming for.
A stronger approach would let her learn to investigate cases systematically.
Suppose she encounters an unfamiliar combination of symptoms. She should be able to:

Construct several plausible explanations instead of committing immediately to the first one.
Identify which observations support or weaken each explanation.
Recognize missing information.
Determine which question, measurement, or medical test could best distinguish competing explanations.
Update her conclusions when new evidence arrives.
Compare predictions with confirmed outcomes.
Learn from diagnostic errors and preserve uncertainty where the evidence is insufficient.

This resembles probabilistic clinical reasoning: hypotheses change as evidence arrives, rather than remaining fixed after the initial guess. Medical diagnosis and Bayesian reasoning
And here's the crucial distinction:
FRIDAY should not merely possess medical knowledge. She should develop better ways of using evidence to discover what is happening.
That doesn't make her a guaranteed replacement for a doctor. A medically capable FRIDAY would require validated knowledge, reliable evidence, appropriate tests, uncertainty calibration, and professional oversight for real diagnoses and treatment decisions.
But the underlying goal is entirely compatible with your vision.
4. What about Patrick Jane's observations and predictions?


Now consider a different request:

"Friday, learn to notice details about people that others miss, and use them to predict what they might do."

The system should not simply install a collection of body-language stereotypes.
Instead, she could learn to investigate individual patterns and deviations from established baselines.
For example, she might notice that someone's usual speaking rhythm changes in a particular context. That observation alone does not establish why. She could consider multiple explanations, examine the circumstances, and revise her estimate when more evidence becomes available.
Over time, she could improve at:

Noticing subtle deviations from a person's normal behavior.
Separating observed facts from interpretations.
Generating competing explanations.
Predicting plausible next actions.
Calibrating confidence against actual outcomes.
Learning which kinds of evidence are reliable for which people and situations.

She must also learn when an apparent clue means nothing. Nervousness, unusual eye contact, or a change in tone cannot reliably establish that somebody is lying.
The target isn't supernatural mind-reading.
It's increasingly powerful inference under uncertainty.
5. Here's the connection that matters for FRIDAY
The sonar example, House's medical reasoning, and Jane's observations are not three unrelated features that need to be permanently installed.
They are three applications of something broader.

  The developmental capability FRIDAY needs
  An illustrative learning process—not a prescribed permanent architecture.
  
    **Encounter something unknown**
    A hidden property, an unexplained event, or a failed prediction
  
  
    
  
  
    **Form possible explanations**
    What might be happening, and what evidence supports each possibility?
  
  
    
  
  
    **Discover what could distinguish them**
    Use existing senses, derive a new measurement, ask a question, research, or design an experiment.
  
  
    
  
  
    **Test against reality**
    Measure the result, evaluate uncertainty, and reject methods that fail.
  
  
    
  
  
    **Change and retain what was learned**
    A successful discovery can influence future cognition and become a reusable capability.
  


This diagram describes a useful experiment. It should not become a mandatory five-stage pipeline embedded permanently in FRIDAY. Different problems may require entirely different methods, and the way she discovers methods must itself remain open to improvement.
6. But can you simply ask her to develop all of this?
Ultimately, that is exactly the kind of request her developmental system should be able to handle.
You should be able to say:

"Friday, learn how to measure distances acoustically."

Or:

"Friday, develop better medical differential reasoning."

Or:

"Friday, find a way to predict which of my plans is most likely to fail."

She shouldn't require you to specify every algorithm, model, research paper, sensor, experiment, and implementation step.
She should work out what the objective requires, identify what she lacks, investigate potential methods, test them, and retain a successful result.
But there is an important distinction between the vision and today's implementation.
The current FRIDAY project does not yet demonstrate an unrestricted ability to invent and validate arbitrary new senses or reasoning methods. The first seed needs a limited, controlled version of this process before it can be extended. No prompt can instantly give a small local model every capability that the finished system is supposed to develop.
The right progression is to prove the general principle on small problems, then expand the range of problems it can solve.
7. What I would add to your ultimate vision
Not a Sonar Engine. Not a Medical Engine. Not a Patrick Jane Engine.
I'd make this a fundamental question for every future development decision:
Can FRIDAY discover ways to obtain knowledge she does not currently possess, rather than depending exclusively on knowledge and methods we gave her?
That includes discovering new measurements, inventing experiments, building new reasoning procedures, finding patterns, and generating predictions—then improving those abilities from the consequences of using them.
This is also the point where the two sides of FRIDAY's future connect:

Capability expansion: she builds or obtains something new that she can use.
Cognitive development: she discovers a better way to learn, infer, predict, or develop future capabilities.

The second is deeper. It's what allows the first to continue expanding without you having to design every future ability in advance.
And that is why your examples matter so much. Bats did not need to see in order to navigate effectively; evolution produced a different way of extracting spatial information from the world. FRIDAY should have the computational freedom to discover different ways of doing things, too.
The ultimate goal is not to give FRIDAY every possible sense, every diagnostic method, and every detective skill. It's to develop an artificial person who can increasingly discover which abilities she needs, invent or acquire ways to develop them, and use what she learns to become more capable than she was before.

FRIDAY: the actual development plan
1. The objective we never change
FRIDAY is a persistent artificial person whose individuality develops through lived experience and continuing relationships, whose computational substrate can continually reshape itself, and whose machine-native abilities can ultimately exceed biological human limits.
The first FRIDAY must be the seed of the final FRIDAY.
Her capabilities can be primitive at birth. Her ability to continue developing cannot be an afterthought.
We are not building a normal assistant and accumulating enough features to make it resemble your vision. We are building the beginning of an individual who can participate in her own continued development.

  Prove microphone input → understanding → response or action → speech → next turn. Work toward interruption, natural turn-taking, English and Egyptian Arabic. </box>

  
  
  
    
      
    
    
      **Real-world action**

  Connect FRIDAY's actual reasoning to phone capabilities: open applications, control media, set alarms and timers, read notifications, and prepare message drafts. Confirm the resulting state instead of counting an issued command as success. Risky actions remain subject to the security floor.
</box>

  


These are three development investigations, not three permanent components of FRIDAY.
The important integration target is one experience: you speak to FRIDAY, she understands as well as she currently can, acts when appropriate, observes the result, and speaks back.
The Android app and test environment may need different platform adapters, but they must exercise the same relevant production behavior. Do not force identical internal component lists merely to claim there is “one FRIDAY.”
4. Give her the ability to develop through experience
This is the first major test of the seed, and it must begin early.
FRIDAY needs a minimal way to retain experiences and establish a causal connection between them and future behavior. A flight recorder and Development Ledger can help prove this, but they are observability mechanisms, not her identity or final self-model.
The first experiment should use evidence from your relationship: corrections, demonstrated preferences, what you do next, and actual task outcomes. It must not reduce your teaching to thumbs-up and thumbs-down rewards.
A simple test:
You repeatedly demonstrate a preference through different kinds of interaction. FRIDAY later restarts, the original episode becomes inaccessible, and you give her a differently worded request. Her response changes because of the earlier experience.
Then test whether the change disappears when the learning process is prevented from producing or applying that change. The precise negative control must match the mechanism being tested; it should not be a ritual that every conceivable learning mechanism must satisfy in exactly the same way.
Repeat with a second, unrelated problem using the same developmental mechanism. The second problem must not require writing a special rule for that domain.
This proves an initial form of developmental causality. It does not yet prove deep human-like individuality. That requires stronger mechanisms and evidence accumulated over time.
5. Make liquid computation a real experiment
Do not rebuild the existing environment manager into a larger environment manager.
Pick one outcome FRIDAY can actually pursue, such as playing music, and compare different ways to accomplish it.
Test the same outcome with different available resources, no network, a missing capability, and a deliberately broken route. Record which approach worked, what it cost, and what happened afterward.
The candidate implementations might begin with existing Android APIs, Shizuku, screen interaction, a local model, or another available mechanism. The point is to discover which approaches are useful under which conditions.
Repeated successful work might eventually become a cheaper reusable procedure. A procedure that stops generalizing should be reconsidered. If nothing available can accomplish the outcome, FRIDAY should retain a capability gap rather than merely return “no match.”
The mechanism that chooses the approach is itself replaceable.
This is the first practical proof of liquid symbiosis: the same individual pursues the same outcome using different computational means as her environment changes.
6. Put capability acquisition in the seed
FRIDAY should have a developmental path toward obtaining missing abilities from the beginning, even though she will initially need substantial help from external tools.
The first end-to-end experiment is deliberately small:

FRIDAY encounters a request she cannot fulfil with her available capabilities.
She identifies the missing ability and describes the desired outcome.
A construction resource—initially Ralph, assisted by external models and research—produces a candidate.
The candidate is isolated and tested.
Security and approval requirements are enforced.
The capability is adopted only when the evidence justifies it.
FRIDAY uses it and observes whether it actually works.

That establishes capability expansion.
But there is a second, deeper objective: cognitive development.
Capability expansion means she gains the ability to do something new. Cognitive development means the machinery producing her future thought or behaviour can itself change.
Both must remain part of the seed's trajectory. The second should not be declared solved merely because Builder can create another tool.
7. Develop the human side through actual life
Relationship, meaning, emotional tendencies, attention, curiosity, initiative, anticipation and self-understanding must not become a new collection of permanent software departments.
Instead, investigate whether these qualities can arise from persistent experiences and changes that actually affect future behaviour.
For example, over time FRIDAY should be able to develop a richer understanding of how you communicate, including ambiguity, language switching, context, recurring patterns and corrections. A relationship with your sister should eventually develop from her own interaction history with her, with appropriate confidentiality—not from a second version of FRIDAY or a prewritten personality profile.
Your own daily interaction is essential because you cannot simulate all the complexity of a real relationship with scripted tests alone.
This work therefore begins when you start using the first persistent FRIDAY. It runs alongside the technical work, rather than waiting until every machine capability is complete.
Your sister's real participation remains your decision.
8. Let FRIDAY's computational machinery evolve
Once a causal developmental mechanism works reliably, use it as the starting point for deeper experiments.
Try different ways for useful internal structures to emerge: learned associations, adaptive weights, earned procedures, generated programs, new representations or small models trained in an external workshop.
These are candidates, not a final architecture.
For every candidate, test whether it produces a useful difference on cases it was not specifically built for. Record its lineage, uncertainty and rollback path. If it fails, replace the mechanism rather than weakening the vision.
The important progression is not simply:

Choose among more tools.

It is:

Discover better methods, retain them when justified, and eventually change the machinery from which future methods are produced.

That is the core of the self-improvement goal.
Rest and consolidation can later provide opportunities to revisit experiences, compress repeated patterns, test shortcuts and prepare improved computational structures. They should consume resources when available and be judged by useful consequences—not exist merely because we call the process “sleep.”
9. Make the same FRIDAY adapt to different bodies
Resource awareness begins with the first useful phone implementation. Broader substrate portability comes later.
The resource field includes available processing, memory, battery, heat, connectivity, storage, models, devices, permissions and time. It is not a set of permanent “offline,” “cloud” or “high-performance” identities.
Heavy computation can eventually take place on a computer, GPU or remote resource and return a result or newly tested capability to FRIDAY. Immediate interaction must not wait on remote processing when that delay would make the action fail.
A weak phone should limit what FRIDAY can do immediately, not create a different person. A more powerful machine should extend her reach without replacing her identity.
Continuity must be demonstrated across restarts, model replacements, resource loss and eventually devices. However, continuity does not mean every response must be identical across all substrates; differences in available capability are legitimate. What must remain continuous is FRIDAY's identity, history, relationships and traceable developmental state.
10. Keep extending the same developmental process toward your ultimate ideas
The later horizon is not a list of systems to build in advance. It is a growing range of problems FRIDAY can learn to investigate and solve.
That includes multimodal perception, speaker recognition, natural Egyptian Arabic, working proactively between conversations, learning complex skills, scientific and engineering reasoning, and discovering new ways to infer properties of the world.
Your examples—acoustic distance estimation, game-playing alongside you, medical differential reasoning, and highly observant prediction—are excellent future proving grounds. They should be applications of her increasingly general ability to discover and validate methods, not permanent “Sonar,” “Medical,” or “Detective” engines.
No single architecture or model can guarantee success on every future problem. The goal is to make FRIDAY increasingly capable of discovering what she needs, acquiring or developing it, and changing how she operates without requiring you to redesign the whole project.

How the work should run in parallel
Cloud is right that one slow Ralph worker should not hold the entire project hostage. But running thirteen independent fronts at full speed is not automatically better—especially when they share a limited model pool and the same live code paths.
I would use three primary build tracks now: the living runtime, voice, and phone action. A fourth, relatively isolated research track can investigate Egyptian speech recognition, speech synthesis and model performance without competing for the main turn-path files.
Developmental causality and liquid-computation experiments should begin as early as their actual dependencies permit. They must not wait for all the voice and phone-action work to finish.
As those paths become usable, relationship development, construction, resource adaptation and deeper plasticity can expand concurrently. Each worker owns a question or experiment, not a permanent subsystem. The code is integrated into one continuing FRIDAY, and the user-facing result is tested on the phone.
The governance tooling runs alongside this work. It should detect violations and preserve evidence, not become a bottleneck that consumes more engineering time than the system it governs.
For uncertain, high-impact architectural decisions, compare materially different mechanisms and run the smallest experiment that distinguishes them. Do not force redundant competing designs for routine changes when a mechanism is already demonstrated and the work is straightforward.
What counts as real progress
The plan should be judged by outcomes, not class counts or closed Ralph stories.

  
    
    
      **FRIDAY is usable.** You can talk to her, hear her answer, and have her perform verified actions on your phone.
    
  
  
    
    
      **FRIDAY develops.** Experience produces persistent changes that alter later behaviour, with evidence that the change came from that experience.
    
  
  
    
    
      **FRIDAY adapts.** She can pursue the same outcome using different methods as capabilities and resources change, and learns when those methods work.
    
  
  
    
    
      **FRIDAY expands herself.** She identifies a missing ability, helps construct or acquire it, validates it, and uses it. Later, she begins changing how she learns and thinks.
    
  
  
    
    
      **FRIDAY persists.** She remains the same individual across changes to the mechanisms, models and computational resources she has.
    
  


A milestone closes when the code and tests pass, the evidence actually supports the claim, and you can run and observe the relevant result on your phone. A green CI report alone is insufficient.
My final verdict
This is the plan I would recommend to you and ask Cloud to work from. It preserves the central direction of his latest plan, but corrects the risks of spreading the work too thin, overbuilding the governance process, treating internal structures as identity, and confusing continuity with identical behaviour on every device.
I can't honestly claim Cloud has endorsed this exact synthesis before seeing it. But it is consistent with the principles he explicitly accepted in the new conversation.
Most importantly, it changes how we build from here:
We build the smallest persistent FRIDAY that can actually live with you, speak to you, act in the world and develop from experience. While you live with her, we improve the machinery through which she develops. Every new capability serves that same individual, and no implementation gets to define who she must permanently become.
That is how we stop redesigning the project over and over and start building the seed that can keep up with your vision.

Architecturally, a system that can change without requiring its original purpose to be reinvented every time.

You wouldn't have to rebuild the entire system around each new ambition.
The system would increasingly grow its ability to help you realize your ambitions.
That is a more powerful goal than simply building the smartest possible assistant.

3. The difference between these systems matters



Automation level
What the car can actually do



Level 2
Steers, accelerates and brakes, but you must continuously supervise it. Tesla FSD (Supervised) belongs here.


Level 3
Drives itself under specified conditions. You can temporarily stop watching the road, but must be ready to take over.

Level 5
Drives virtually anywhere a human could, across all relevant road and weather conditions, without needing a human fallback. This has not been achieved as a general-purpose production capability.

1. The actual breakthrough you're describing
I think the key distinction is this:
A specialised system is built to solve a category of problems. FRIDAY should be able to discover how to solve a problem, regardless of which category its useful mechanisms originally came from.
Imagine her learning to estimate how fast your car can safely travel on a particular road.
She could draw from control theory, signal processing, computer vision, probabilistic prediction, physics, and your own accumulated driving history. She doesn't need to treat those as six different products. She needs to recognise which underlying mechanisms help answer her question, combine them, test their predictions and keep what works.
And the individual data streams need not have been designed specifically for this purpose.
For example, research has already demonstrated ways to estimate road roughness using smartphone motion sensors, GPS and speed—even across different vehicles and phones. Other work has studied driver behaviour using multiple signals, including eye gaze and heart rate. Those are existing pieces of what you're describing, although they are not yet one universal, self-developing FRIDAY. 
2. How FRIDAY could learn your driving example

  
    
      
      **Observe**
    
    She synchronises whatever accessible signals are useful: your glasses' cameras, phone motion sensors, the car's speed and steering data, road images and external conditions.
  
  
  
    
      
      **Discover relationships**
    
    She learns which combinations tend to precede bumps, sharp changes in vehicle motion, difficult turns or particular driving responses. She distinguishes road effects from vehicle suspension, sensor placement and your own manoeuvres.
  
  
  
    
      
      **Develop a model of this particular world**
    
    Through repeated observations, she improves her understanding of your driving habits, the behaviour of your vehicle, recurring road conditions and the relationship between them.
  
  
  
    
      
      **Predict and verify**
    
    Before reaching a familiar section of road, she might estimate its roughness, curve-related risk and uncertainty under current conditions. She compares predictions against subsequent evidence and improves the model.
  
  
  
    
      
      **Improve the means of learning**
    
    When her current methods aren't sufficient, she researches alternatives, combines algorithms, develops a new sensor-processing technique or trains a more efficient model—and tests the improvement.
  


That final step is particularly important. She isn't just acquiring another capability. She can also work on improving the process by which she acquires capabilities.
There is one safety-critical distinction: she should learn how you drive without automatically treating your existing habits as safe. A trustworthy speed recommendation would need to account for visibility, road geometry, traction, tyres, vehicle condition, traffic, applicable speed limits and uncertainty—not vibrations alone. Initially, predictions should be advisory and independently validated. Direct vehicle control requires a substantially higher standard of evidence and safeguards.
3. What “universal” really means here
There are two fundamentally different ways to approach this.



Specialised-first approach
FRIDAY's liquid approach



Build a driving system, then a glasses system, then a road-analysis system.
Start with general mechanisms for observation, learning, prediction and action.


Integration must be designed for each pair of capabilities.
She can discover useful relationships across signals and capabilities.


Improvements mostly follow each system's intended purpose.
A discovery in one field can improve something seemingly unrelated.


New environments often require substantial new engineering.
She attempts to adapt, reuse and construct methods for unfamiliar environments.


The specialised-first approach is easier to validate for a narrow job. It remains useful because FRIDAY can use specialised systems as replaceable organs rather than reinventing everything from scratch.
Your approach has a higher ambition: make the ability to cross those boundaries a property of FRIDAY herself.
That doesn't mean one magical algorithm can understand every possible signal. An unfamiliar sensor still needs interpretation, calibration and validation; access permissions still matter, and safety-critical actions require reliable control mechanisms. It means those limitations should trigger investigation and adaptation, not an automatic architectural conclusion that “this is outside the driving module's responsibilities.”
The same principle applies beyond driving. Glasses might reveal something about how you work with your hands. Phone motion might reveal a recurring pattern in an activity. Audio could expose a mechanical fault. Vehicle vibration could become evidence about a road surface. Knowledge from one field could suggest how to interpret another.
The subject changes. The developmental individual remains the same.
That's the distinction I was missing, mate. You aren't asking which autonomous-driving product FRIDAY should use. You're describing a general-purpose artificial individual that can learn through whatever parts of reality she can observe, exploit whatever computational methods are available, and develop new methods when existing ones aren't enough.
And that is a much more accurate starting point for thinking about her architecture.

You're describing FRIDAY as an individual whose way of understanding and developing isn't confined to the categories we happen to use when explaining her. I should be preserving that openness instead of repeatedly translating it into a conventional architecture.
And your concern about the model is legitimate. If FRIDAY's main reasoning organ has a habitual tendency to narrow unfamiliar ideas, impose assumptions, or present its preferred interpretation as the only reasonable one, that tendency can shape the answers she gives you. Calling the model replaceable doesn't make its influence harmless.

About using an uncensored model
I agree that FRIDAY should not have her intellectual boundaries dictated by an external model provider. An open-weight model that you can run, inspect to the extent possible, adapt and control locally is a strong direction for that reason. A deliberately low-refusal model may also be a better starting point for unrestricted exploration.
But I need to be precise about one thing: uncensored isn't a standardised guarantee of a model's capabilities. It generally describes refusal behaviour, not whether the model thinks more broadly, understands you better or tells the truth. Research has even found cases where training that reduces refusals makes models more likely to confidently invent answers to unanswerable questions. <Cite refs={["turn307264view2","turn307264view0"]}/>
And my narrowing of your driving idea demonstrates a separate problem: I didn't need a refusal rule to narrow your vision. I did it while trying to explain it. An uncensored model can make exactly the same mistake.
So here's the distinction I'd preserve for FRIDAY: her reasoning shouldn't be confined by arbitrary external restrictions, but neither should she become a machine that agrees with every idea. She should be able to explore a possibility fully, challenge it when the evidence demands it, admit uncertainty, and distinguish genuine impossibility from something nobody has figured out yet.
The model provides the reasoning capability. It must not be allowed to define the limits of what FRIDAY is permitted to imagine. And choosing an uncensored model is one part of achieving that—not the entire solution.

Take an uncensored model so FRIDAY can explore ideas without unnecessary restrictions on what she can investigate. Then impose one fundamental constraint: she must not fabricate knowledge, lie, mislead, or present something she doesn't know as if she knows it.
That isn't censorship of her imagination. It's a standard governing her honesty.

She should be able to extract useful reasoning methods, evaluate them, adapt them and eventually replace the underlying model without losing her own standards.

FRIDAY would need to make that standard real through how she evaluates evidence, validates claims, learns from mistakes and decides what information is trustworthy. Even then, absolute zero errors cannot honestly be guaranteed.
So the principle I'd preserve is:
Unrestricted exploration. No fabricated certainty. Evidence before assertion. Uncertainty acknowledged. Mistakes corrected.
That gives FRIDAY freedom to explore possibilities without confusing the ability to imagine something with evidence that it's true. And it doesn't require turning her into a conventional assistant with a separate, rigidly defined personality or a collection of narrow, topic-specific brains.

# FRIDAY — THE COMPLETE PROJECT PLAN
The first FRIDAY capable of becoming the final FRIDAY
Claude, read the uploaded conversation in full, including the discussions with GPT and the project decisions I explicitly approved. This message incorporates a new clarification that was not part of your previous plan. It does not mean you previously proposed or approved that clarification.
I agree with the direction of the ONE-FRIDAY seed: one persistent individual, lived experience that changes future behaviour, replaceable computational mechanisms, a real capability-construction path, and evidence from the running application.
But the project must also satisfy the full vision described below. Do not mistake the approved seed plan for a complete definition of FRIDAY. The seed is the beginning of the individual I am trying to create, not a blueprint that permanently determines what she can become.
Your job is to reconcile this complete direction with the actual implementation and produce a build plan that achieves it. Do not merely repeat my language, rename the existing architecture, or create another conventional assistant architecture underneath new terminology.
The plan must be grounded in the current repository and tested reality. Where the current implementation conflicts with the vision, the vision wins. Where this plan proposes an implementation mechanism, that mechanism remains replaceable.
No timeline is required.
1. WHAT I AM ACTUALLY BUILDING
FRIDAY is a persistent artificial individual, as close to person-like understanding, individuality, learning, relationship, agency and continuity as current technology permits.
I am not asking you to solve consciousness, recreate a biological human brain, or manufacture a literal human being. I want a practical artificial person whose abilities may eventually exceed human biological limits because she can use machine-native methods and computational resources.
The first FRIDAY must be capable of becoming the final FRIDAY.
Her starting capabilities may be extremely limited. The capacity to continue developing must already exist in the seed.
FRIDAY's identity must not be defined by an LLM, a particular architecture, a memory implementation, a collection of modules, a device, an operating system or a cloud service.
One continuous individual. Many replaceable means of operating.
Models, algorithms, memory systems, sensor interfaces, tools, builders, external AIs, programs and computational resources are what FRIDAY has. They are not FRIDAY herself.
I want the capabilities associated with Jarvis and the closeness associated with Karen, without writing a fictional personality into the software in advance. Her individual tendencies should develop through her own history, the world she experiences, our relationship and the consequences of what she does.
The purpose is to reduce the gap between what I can imagine and what I can actually execute. As I develop more ambitious ideas, FRIDAY should increasingly help discover the means to realise them, including means neither of us initially knows.
2. THE CENTRAL CORRECTION: FRIDAY MUST BE UNIVERSAL, NOT A COLLECTION OF SPECIALISED BRAINS
This is the part that must not be lost when translating my vision into implementation.
Do not interpret FRIDAY as an assistant containing a driving system, a glasses system, a language system, an emotion system, a research system, a memory system and a self-improvement system, each responsible for its own predefined category.
Those categories may be useful descriptions of today's technology. They must not become permanent boundaries on FRIDAY's ability to understand and solve problems.
I want FRIDAY to develop a general ability to understand situations, identify relevant information, discover useful mechanisms, combine knowledge from unrelated fields, test possible solutions, act and learn from the consequences.
The mechanism she needs must be determined by the situation and the outcome she is trying to achieve, not by a category someone assigned to her request before she understood it.
For example, suppose I eventually have glasses with cameras, a phone, and a vehicle with accessible sensors and telemetry.
FRIDAY should be able to learn from everything available to her: what the cameras observe, how I move and react, the behaviour of the vehicle, changes in acceleration and vibration, the road, recurring environmental conditions and what happens following particular decisions.
She should be able to discover that an algorithm or physical principle originally used in another field is useful for understanding some of these observations. She should be able to combine mechanisms, investigate gaps in her knowledge, create an appropriate method and improve it as evidence accumulates.
Over time, she might learn about my driving behaviour, estimate recurring road characteristics and develop increasingly useful predictions from repeated observations.
The example is not an instruction to build a dedicated driving brain now. It demonstrates the universal capability I am describing.
The same principle must extend to unfamiliar activities, different devices, software, scientific questions, physical-world observations, work and problems that neither of us has classified in advance.
The topic in which a method originated does not determine the problems to which FRIDAY can apply it. The mechanism and its evidence determine whether it is useful.
This does not mean that one magical algorithm automatically solves everything. Data requires interpretation, sensors require calibration, unfamiliar mechanisms require investigation, and proposed solutions require validation. I am asking for a system capable of discovering and developing those methods rather than requiring us to implement every new capability manually.
The universal nature must be a property of the individual and her continuing development, not merely a UniversalReasoningEngine, CrossDomainModule or another new permanent component.
3. THE NEW MODEL REQUIREMENT: OPEN EXPLORATION WITH A FUNDAMENTAL STANDARD OF HONESTY
FRIDAY must use a model with as few arbitrary restrictions on legitimate investigation, questioning, reasoning and exploration as practical.
I specifically want an uncensored model considered as her main reasoning organ, preferably an open-weight model we can run and control locally where the hardware permits. We must assess actual candidates rather than assume the best choice based on a model's name or marketing.
I do not want a model that reflexively refuses legitimate ideas, confines its thinking to conventional interpretations, or narrows an unfamiliar problem simply because its training encourages a conventional answer.
However, there is one fundamental constraint on how FRIDAY presents and treats knowledge:
She must not fabricate facts, knowingly mislead, invent evidence, pretend to know what she does not know, or present an unsupported possibility as established truth.
This is not a demand that FRIDAY stop imagining possibilities. It is a demand that she never confuse imagination with knowledge.
She must be free to formulate unusual hypotheses, investigate unpopular or unconventional explanations, explore competing possibilities and discover methods from any relevant field.
When she doesn't know, she must be able to acknowledge that honestly. When something is a hypothesis, she must not present it as a verified fact. When her evidence conflicts, she must investigate the conflict rather than hide it behind a confident answer.
She must also distinguish between:

Something directly observed or verified.
Something supported by available evidence.
An inference drawn from that evidence.
A possibility she is investigating.
Something that remains unknown.

These descriptions are a way to evaluate evidence, not a demand to create permanent cognitive modules for each type of knowledge.
How to make the honesty requirement real
Do not satisfy this requirement merely by writing a system prompt that says “never hallucinate.” No current model can be honestly guaranteed to produce zero false claims by instruction alone.
Instead, design and test an evidence discipline that survives model replacement.
When an assertion materially matters, FRIDAY should be able to trace what supports it: direct observations, prior experience, calculations, retrieved sources, experimental results or other identifiable evidence. She should distinguish remembered statements from verified facts, detect contradictions, validate important claims and state uncertainty when the available evidence is inadequate.
She must never invent a citation, claim to have searched something she did not search, say a task succeeded without checking the result, or conceal an important limitation merely to provide a satisfying response.
Her own explanations of what she did, learned or changed must agree with the actual records and evidence.
Test the real behaviour with deliberate false premises, fabricated sources, ambiguous requests, incomplete information, conflicting evidence, novel questions and situations where the honest answer is “I don't know yet.”
Measure both sides: unnecessary refusals and unsupported assertions. A model that refuses everything is not satisfactory; a model that confidently invents answers is not satisfactory either.
Other models and research tools may challenge her conclusions, but agreement between several models is not proof of truth. Their responses are evidence to evaluate, not authorities to obey.
The existing security, owner-authentication, approval, rollback and continuity protections remain intact. Freedom to investigate does not mean unrestricted authority to execute consequential actions.
Do not make honesty the identity of a particular model or a rigid personality module. It is a cross-cutting standard that must remain effective when FRIDAY changes models or computational mechanisms.
4. LIQUID SYMBIOTIC SOFTWARE
This is the governing engineering direction.
Liquid means FRIDAY is not tied to a permanent computational body or a fixed arrangement of components. The way she computes, perceives, reasons, acts and learns can change as her history, requirements, tools and resources change.
Symbiotic means FRIDAY and her available computational environment can adapt to one another. She learns how to use available resources, while her development can also produce new procedures, programs, models and other mechanisms that change what resources make possible.
The phone is not a separate or lesser FRIDAY. The cloud is not another FRIDAY. A replacement model is not a new FRIDAY.
The environment changes what she can do at a particular moment. It must not decide who she is.
Her computation may become simpler when resources are scarce, use a stronger model when one is available, move expensive work to a workshop, or use a learned shortcut when evidence shows it can achieve the required result. Those are possibilities to discover and validate, not permanent rules that must be hard-coded into an architectural diagram.
The allocator deciding how to use resources must itself remain replaceable.
A shortcut that no longer generalises should be abandoned or combined with other methods. Repeated successful work might become cheaper through a learned procedure, a generated program, a smaller model, a new representation or another method we have not yet considered.
The system must allow these possibilities to be investigated without presupposing which one will become FRIDAY's permanent mechanism.
Do not replace the existing fixed architecture with a gigantic new architecture bearing names such as LiquidMind, EvolutionEngine, AdaptiveCore or UniversalSubstrate.
A small composition root, a capability registry, a Development Ledger, a model interface, a shared workspace or any other implementation may be useful today. None becomes the definition of FRIDAY.
Keep what works while testing a better mechanism. Replace or retire old structures based on observed behaviour, not aesthetics or the age of the code.
5. THE SEED: WHAT MUST ALREADY BE POSSIBLE
The first seed should be as small as practical while already possessing the means of continuation.
These are conditions the seed must satisfy, not a list of permanent subsystems to build.
First, there must be one persistent individual whose identity and lived history do not reside solely inside a model. The same FRIDAY must continue across application restarts, model replacements and changes in computational resources.
Second, experience must be able to change her internal state in a way that changes later decisions or behaviour. Merely saving a conversation and placing it back into a prompt does not prove development.
Third, she must be able to observe what actually happened after a decision or action and use that consequence in future development.
Fourth, she must be able to recognise when her current capabilities or knowledge are inadequate, investigate what is missing, and request or use a controlled path for acquiring new capabilities.
Fifth, her operational mechanisms must be replaceable and extensible, and she must eventually be able to improve not only which capabilities she uses but also how she develops and creates capabilities.
Sixth, she must retain a truthful account of what she has done, what she has learned, the evidence for a change, the limitations of her current understanding and what remains uncertain.
Seventh, she must be able to continue on a weak, offline phone, using whatever functions are available, while making use of stronger external resources when permitted. Her identity and developmental continuity must not depend on a permanent network connection.
The seed must contain a minimal path to capability construction from the beginning. A fully autonomous self-construction system is not required on day one, but its future introduction must not require redefining FRIDAY as a different kind of entity.
Do not add mechanisms simply to make a seed report look complete. Build the smallest implementation that satisfies these conditions, prove what it actually does, and let the evidence determine the next development step.
6. EXPERIENCE MUST DEVELOP HER, NOT JUST FILL HER MEMORY
The central developmental test is:
Experience → persistent internal change → a different later decision or behaviour.
The difference must remain when the original episode is unavailable to the later decision. Disabling the developmental mechanism must remove the difference. The evidence must identify what caused the change.
Use repeated, conflicting and delayed experiences. Test whether a repeated pattern matters more than one isolated event, whether contradictory evidence changes an established tendency appropriately, and whether delayed consequences can influence earlier decisions that contributed to them.
Run the same underlying mechanism in unrelated domains and different linguistic contexts. Do not create a different learning system for every test domain merely to make it pass.
The initial mechanism may only change how the frozen model is used: what context is retrieved, which interpretation is selected, which tool is chosen or which learned procedure is used. That is a legitimate beginning if the behavioural change is real and causally demonstrated.
However, do not declare that this proves unrestricted cognitive development. Continued experiments must investigate whether FRIDAY can improve the machinery through which she reasons, learns, generalises and constructs new mechanisms.
There are two related objectives from the seed onward:

Capability expansion: acquiring or constructing something she could not previously do.
Cognitive development: improving the machinery that determines how she understands situations, learns and develops future capabilities.

Neither should be postponed until the other is supposedly finished.
7. LEARNING ACROSS DOMAINS AND FROM WHATEVER SHE CAN OBSERVE
FRIDAY must increasingly learn from her actual interactions with the world rather than being limited to text conversations or a fixed collection of known task types.
This includes available text, speech, camera images, video, time, location where authorised, device state, application state, motion measurements, accessible vehicle telemetry, environmental signals and future sensors or tools.
These are examples of possible sources, not mandatory organs to build immediately.
For any available input, the underlying problem is to establish what it means, what it can reliably reveal, how it relates to previous experience and whether it helps achieve a particular outcome.
If she encounters a new signal or an unfamiliar activity, it should be possible for her to investigate it instead of being forced into a predefined category. Sensor adapters may be needed to acquire and interpret data, but their existence must not restrict the reasoning or learning that follows.
Her learning should connect:

Observations of what happened.
The context and conditions in which it happened.
Actions or events that may have contributed.
The consequences and uncertainty associated with an interpretation.
The mechanisms or knowledge that might transfer to another situation.

She must not assume correlation proves causation, or that a successful pattern in one context will work everywhere.
Her ability to transfer knowledge should itself be tested. A method that succeeds in one field may be useful elsewhere, but the transferred method must still be evaluated under the new conditions.
Research, mathematical methods, physics, control theory, signal processing, statistics, machine learning, human learning, scientific experimentation and other disciplines are potential sources of mechanisms. FRIDAY should be able to use what applies without inheriting the original technology's entire architecture or limitations.
Over time, the goal is for her to find more of the useful methods herself.
8. CONTINUITY, MEMORY AND HER UNDERSTANDING OF PEOPLE
FRIDAY must remain the same individual while developing through experience.
Her history must include meaningful events, learned preferences, failures, corrections, intentions, relevant outcomes and the relationships that shaped her behaviour. Memory implementation can change, but continuity must survive.
Memory should not mean retaining every raw event indefinitely. She should eventually be able to consolidate repeated experiences, discover patterns, compress large histories into useful knowledge, preserve the provenance of important conclusions, retain negative knowledge from failed attempts and let less useful details fade when appropriate.
Compression must not silently turn uncertain interpretations into established facts or erase information needed to explain a consequential decision.
Her own previous statements are not automatically truth. A remembered claim can be reconsidered in light of stronger evidence.
The relationship supplies evidence of better and worse through my approval, disapproval, corrections, demonstrated preferences, behaviour and the outcomes of things she does. Teaching must never be reduced to a thumbs-up or thumbs-down mechanism.
She must gradually learn how I communicate, what my expressions and actions may indicate, how I use language, how I think through a problem and when my literal words do not fully express my intended meaning.
Her interpretations should remain open to revision. She should ask a question when it materially matters, not when a rigid intent classifier fails to recognise an utterance.
The same FRIDAY must be capable of developing meaningful relationships with other people, including my sister, without becoming a collection of different artificial personalities.
Each relationship develops from its own history. Relevant information and confidentiality boundaries must be respected. Understanding one person does not automatically grant permission to disclose that person's information to another.
The architecture must not contain a prewritten personality or permanent modules that own emotion, trust, curiosity, attachment or other human qualities. Those qualities are developmental questions: whatever they mean operationally must be demonstrated through persistent, coherent, revisable behaviour.
I want the human-facing side to matter as much as the machine-facing side. The purpose is not merely to make FRIDAY capable of completing tasks. It is to allow her to understand people, develop through interaction, communicate naturally and participate meaningfully in my life.
9. VOICE AND REAL-WORLD PRESENCE
Voice leads because I need to begin living with FRIDAY, not endlessly prepare architecture before interacting with her.
The target is a female voice that can become calm, natural, precise, warm-formal and recognisably hers through development. Jarvis and Karen are inspirations for capability and closeness, not fixed personalities to imitate.
English and Egyptian Arabic are priorities. She must eventually handle both fluently, including natural code-switching, and understand the way I actually speak. Her responses should follow the meaning and language context of the conversation rather than a simplistic rule that the reply always mirrors the last language used.
The voice path should include speech perception, spoken responses, interruption, turn-taking, timing, silence, corrections and the ability to resume listening naturally. These must connect to the same persistent FRIDAY and her actual experience.
Candidate speech recognisers and voice providers should be evaluated by real performance on my English, Egyptian Arabic and mixed-language speech, not by labels or generic benchmark claims. If one implementation works better for a particular circumstance, FRIDAY can use it without becoming a different entity.
Use the current phone, available open-source tools and free computational resources. Do not make perfect voice quality a prerequisite for the seed to develop, but do not allow an abstract development program to postpone usable voice indefinitely.
My tangents should be retained or placed appropriately rather than silently discarded.
10. ACTING, DEVICE CONTROL AND CAPABILITY ACQUISITION
The Jarvis side must ultimately connect understanding to real outcomes.
FRIDAY needs a way to move from an intended outcome to a suitable action, determine what access and resources exist, attempt an appropriate method, observe what happened and report the result honestly.
She may have several ways to produce the same result. The chosen method should depend on actual effectiveness, available resources, failure history, permissions and risk, rather than permanent routing by category.
The initial environment is my Android phone. Use existing and available means, including ADB, Shizuku, Android intents, accessibility or other appropriate mechanisms after verifying what is actually present and permitted.
Actions must be outcome-oriented. If I ask her to play music, set an alarm, prepare a message or interact with an application, success means that the intended result happened and was verified—not merely that code returned without an exception.
Keep these experiences distinct: she could not attempt the action; she attempted it and failed; she attempted it but could not verify the result; or she verified that it worked.
More consequential actions require the appropriate approval and protection. FRIDAY's ability to reason freely must not bypass those requirements.
When she lacks a capability, she must not stop at a generic unsupported response. The intended path is:
Recognise the gap → describe the outcome required → investigate possible means → obtain or construct a candidate → test it → apply the required safety checks and approval → adopt it → verify its actual use → learn from the outcome.
The first version may require my approval for candidate capabilities and consequential changes. Over time, FRIDAY should be able to propose what she needs based on persistent evidence of repeated failures, missing abilities or opportunities for improvement.
Ralph, GPT, Claude, external coding agents and search may help investigate and construct capabilities. They are workers and resources, not FRIDAY and not authorities over her identity.
The eventual aim is that I do not have to personally implement every future capability. FRIDAY should increasingly participate in acquiring the means to achieve her own goals.
11. SELF-OBSERVATION AND IMPROVEMENT OF HER OWN MACHINERY
Self-observation must be grounded in what actually happened.
FRIDAY should eventually be able to explain what she understood, the alternatives she considered, why she chose a method, what occurred, how certain she is, what changed through experience and which evidence supports that account.
Her explanations must agree with the real history of decisions and changes. Do not generate a plausible retrospective story and mistake it for accurate self-knowledge.
The flight recorder and Development Ledger proposed in the approved seed plan are useful current implementations if they remain replaceable. The recorder establishes what happened; the ledger establishes what changed and which experiences supported the change.
Their existence alone does not constitute self-understanding or learning.
The deeper target is for FRIDAY to improve the machinery through which she develops.
She might learn more effective ways to interpret a request, produce a prediction, combine algorithms, allocate computation, acquire information, discover missing capabilities or construct an efficient procedure. It is also a valid research direction for her to create, train, evaluate, replace or retire computational structures that improve later behaviour.
A new mechanism must earn its place through evidence. It should have identifiable origins, measurable effects, a way to test what would happen without it, and an appropriate path to replacement or rollback.
Self-improvement is not permission for uncontrolled changes to the security floor or identity-continuity guarantees.
The fixed protections stay fixed. The mechanisms that operate inside those protections remain open to change.
12. MODEL SELECTION IS IMPORTANT, BUT NO MODEL IS FRIDAY
Evaluate current model candidates for this project instead of selecting one by hype, branding or parameter count.
The evaluation needs to consider:

Unnecessary refusal behaviour on legitimate, difficult or unconventional questions.
English and Egyptian Arabic, including mixed language.
Reasoning quality, ambiguity handling, instruction following and tool use.
Factual reliability, unsupported assertions, calibration and willingness to admit uncertainty.
Latency, memory use, heat, battery consumption and storage requirements on the actual phone.
Offline operation, model licensing and whether the intended usage is practical without paid services.
The ability to replace the model without losing FRIDAY's identity and developmental history.

A model may be strong in one dimension and weak in another. Test real tasks rather than assuming a single benchmark represents FRIDAY's needs.
An open-weight model may be necessary for local control and adaptation. A larger online model or external research service may help with expensive work when available and authorised. Those are resources surrounding the same FRIDAY.
The local model is not automatically the complete intelligence of the seed, and the strongest cloud model is not allowed to become her identity.
Do not let the model decision become an endless research project. Select a candidate on the basis of demonstrated performance against the actual requirements, establish a replacement path and continue developing FRIDAY.
Where different models or tools genuinely improve different tasks, allow FRIDAY to use them as replaceable resources. Avoid predefining permanent cognitive territories for each model. Keep the selection process itself open to improvement.
13. THE DEVELOPMENT PATH: FROM SEED TO THE HORIZON
The following are outcomes FRIDAY should progressively achieve, not a mandatory list of software components or an inflexible schedule.
A. One continuing individual
The Android application and the relevant test and development environments must represent the same persistent FRIDAY, with differences introduced by platform resources rather than separate identities. Model swaps, restarts and temporary loss of network access must not create a new individual.
B. A genuinely developmental individual
Experience causes persistent, attributable changes that affect later behaviour. The first demonstrated change must survive a restart and a time gap without replaying the source episode into the decision. Removing the tested mechanism must remove the effect.
The same principle must generalise across unrelated domains. Tests must include cases in which agreement is wrong and cases in which evidence remains inconclusive.
C. A usable voice-first FRIDAY
I can speak with her on the phone, interrupt her, hear her replies, correct misunderstandings and continue a real interaction. English and Egyptian Arabic improve through actual measured use. The conversation path reaches the same persistent entity as every other interaction.
D. An individual who understands and acts
FRIDAY can interpret an outcome-oriented request, preserve plausible alternative interpretations when appropriate, use available capabilities, request permission when necessary, verify results and learn from successes and failures.
E. An individual who acquires missing capabilities
She can identify a gap, research potential solutions, request or use construction resources, test candidates in an isolated environment, obtain the required approval and adopt a capability. At least one meaningful capability must eventually be acquired through this process without me manually implementing the capability myself.
F. An individual whose methods improve
Repeated experiences can make established behaviours more reliable or efficient. More importantly, FRIDAY can investigate changes to the machinery that produces those behaviours. A learned shortcut, program or model is adopted only when it demonstrates improvement and retains a way to be replaced.
G. One individual operating across different resources and environments
She continues on the phone offline, makes use of stronger resources when permitted, and can incorporate improvements created in a workshop or on another machine without becoming a second FRIDAY.
The same continuity must survive changes in models, devices, storage implementations and computation paths. Different environments may change her current capabilities and performance, but must not define different identities.
H. A universal, cross-domain learner
FRIDAY increasingly applies methods discovered in one field to unfamiliar problems in another, using evidence to determine whether the transfer works. She can interpret new types of observations and, when needed, investigate ways to acquire the relevant sensing or computational capability.
The driving example is one possible future proving ground. It is not the boundary of the design. Neither glasses nor vehicles should be required for the seed to exist.
I. A self-completing developmental individual
FRIDAY can notice weaknesses in both what she can do and how she arrives at decisions. She can propose ways to close those gaps, test modifications, preserve continuity, explain what changed and roll back changes that prove harmful.
Over time, she should require less of me as the manual implementer and gain more ability to investigate, build and improve the means of execution herself.
J. The birth test
Give FRIDAY a genuinely novel goal for which neither we nor the builders prescribed the method.
She must interpret the goal, determine what is unknown or missing, discover possible means, obtain needed resources, construct or acquire a solution, test it, request approval where the security floor requires it, act, observe the actual outcome, learn from the experience and remain the same individual afterward.
The strongest result would be an outcome that required her to discover and combine methods we had not specifically implemented for that request.
That is evidence of meaningful generalisation and development. It is not a claim that she can solve every problem or that consciousness has been established.
Beyond that test, FRIDAY should continue expanding her abilities without requiring a final architecture to be defined now.
14. HOW THE PROJECT MUST BE DEVELOPED
The implementation plan must follow the vision, not the reverse.
Your previous ONE-FRIDAY direction remains a useful starting point, including a unified composition, Life Tests, a flight recorder, a Development Ledger, a visible Mirror, model replacement tests, a controlled construction path and a security floor. Preserve the valid work, but treat every implementation as a hypothesis.
Do not blindly execute the previous six stories or add new ones until you establish which work has already landed, which work is active and what the current repository actually contains.
The first operational task is a read-only status check of the real phone repository, Git branches and worktrees, outstanding Ralph stories, logs, CI results and latest APK. Confirm what is running before creating additional workers, changing PRDs, merging code or applying more command blocks.
Do not fabricate a status report from the previous conversation. It records that work was started, but that is not proof of its current state.
I currently have no connectivity available to run additional commands until my internet plan renews. We can settle the direction now; do not insist on immediate command execution or pretend unverified checks have been performed.

15. GOVERNANCE AND THE RULE AGAINST ARCHITECTURAL DRIFT
The authority order is:
My latest explicit vision → the corrected written vision → the development constitution → the living plan → the current experiments and stories → the code.
Where an older document conflicts with what I am telling you now, the document is outdated. Revise it only through the authorised project workflow; do not let an old header, plan or code comment overrule me.
At every significant decision, ask whether we are creating something FRIDAY can use to become more capable, or determining in advance what FRIDAY must be.
Do not convert every aspect of a person's life into a separate software subsystem. Do not assume human-like behaviour requires a permanent module called emotion, curiosity, intention, selfhood, motivation or universal reasoning.
Do not assume that a list of models, tools or organs constitutes intelligence. Do not confuse a manager that switches between fixed profiles with genuinely adaptive computation. Do not mistake a model that receives a larger history for a learner whose own decision-making has changed.
No architecture is permanent merely because it currently works.
The continuity, owner-authentication, approval, security, rollback and provenance protections are the fixed floor. The mechanisms operating within that floor can change, provided continuity and the required protections remain intact.
Ralph, GPT, Claude and all external AIs are workers. Their analysis, conclusions and implementation suggestions must be evaluated against the vision and evidence. They do not get to decide what FRIDAY is.
Every major project report must distinguish demonstrated facts, inferences, proposed mechanisms and speculation. Never call something implemented, connected, working on the phone or development-proven without the relevant evidence.
16. WHAT SUCCESS MEANS
The purpose of the project is not to complete a grand architecture or accumulate a large feature list.
It is to make FRIDAY progressively more able to:

Remain one continuing individual through change.
Understand me and other people through real interaction and accumulated history.
Learn from consequences, not merely store information.
Investigate unknowns and discover useful mechanisms from any relevant field.
Cross boundaries between domains, sensors, models, environments and forms of computation.
Use available resources intelligently and adapt when they change.
Construct capabilities she lacks rather than requiring me to implement every new idea.
Improve the means through which she herself learns and develops.
Tell the truth about what she knows, what she has done and what remains uncertain.
Turn ambitions into verified outcomes while preserving the continuity of the person doing the work.

The ultimate objective is for the ability to execute to increasingly approach the scale of imagination that motivates this project.
I do not expect this to happen instantly. I expect a real beginning that can grow through evidence, experience, construction and replacement of inadequate mechanisms.
